**The share is stated before it is committed to (added 2026\-09\-04).** A mid\-cycle apportioned expense reserves at a visibly higher rate than its average monthly cost — €300 rather than €100 — and meeting that as an unexplained deduction is the kind of surprise §2.3 exists to prevent. The fix is not to soften the mechanism but to give the figure in the editor before saving: *"Sets aside €300 a month until Jul 15, then €100 a month — fewer months to spread the first one over, so the full €1,200 is still there when it lands."* A settled expense states one figure and stops. Plain fact, no imperative, no warning register (§2.2, §2.4) — and it leaves a different due date or amount an easy choice rather than a correction.

Deleting **after** the relevant due date has passed shows no prompt and reverses nothing — the real\-world charge is presumed to have happened. Quick\-add on Savings remains the general\-purpose fallback either way (§3.6).

**Removed from quick\-add's surface list (§3.6)** — there's no manual occurrence left to log once sweeps happen automatically.

## 4\. Navigation & Screens

### 4\.1 Top\-level tabs (Decided, 2026\-07\-18 — via `app-register-layout` \+ `layout-architecture-core`)

**At 600dp and above the tabs are a rail 88dp wide, icon over label (Decided 2026\-09\-26, with the owner — closes §13.1 item 5).** Measured on the real layout: a 240dp rail with labels beside the icons leaves Budget one column below a 1192\-wide window, so the default window would lose its second column. ~~**Open check, not a decision:** at 150% text the 88 rail's labels wrap to two lines (76dp of label room, 56 in compact); whether Windows' text size reaches Compose Desktop at all has not been verified (§13.3).~~ **The rail's labels share one size that fits, and the rail scrolls when five cells outgrow the height (Decided 2026\-09\-26, with the owner)** — the tab bar's rule from §2.5, after a phone in landscape at 150% clipped *"Setting"* and ran the rail past a 411dp screen.

**Budget · Activity · Learn · Reports · Settings.**

**On a phone, Settings leaves the bottom bar (Decided 2026\-10\-10, with the owner).** The bar holds Budget · Activity · Learn · Reports; Settings is a gear glyph at the right end of each of those four pages' title row, level with the title (`TabTitle`, `ui/components/Rows.kt`). Only the four tab pages carry it — pushed pages keep just their back arrow — and Settings itself has none. The desktop rail keeps all five, so a wide window draws no glyph. **Back on Settings returns to the page it was opened from** (owner, 2026\-10\-10), after first leaving any of Settings' own sub\-pages; reselecting Settings keeps that origin. From any other tab root, Back is the host's, as before.

Activity was promoted out of Budget's segmented control into its own top\-level tab. It was outgrowing that slot: full account CRUD, quick\-add, and the eventual transaction ledger carry as much weight as Envelopes/Categories, not a third view nested a tap deeper. Placed immediately after Budget since both are "the money itself," while Learn/Reports are reflective.

### 4\.2 Per\-screen structure (Decided, from the register\-layout audit; detail screens added 2026\-09\-02)

Pattern names are defined in §2.4. The **Pushes to** column names the detail and editor screens each surface must reach — previously absent, which left §3.6's Savings full editor, the Account detail screen hosting §9.2's panel, and §7.3's ledger with no stated entry point anywhere in the document.

| Screen | Section order | Pattern | Primary emphasis | Pushes to | Why (tied to §2.4's register) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Budget → Envelopes | ~~Savings (\+ general "\+" and, if Income on, "↓") → Income (if enabled) → Recurring Expenses summary, with the recurring\-threshold note directly under it if applicable (§2.5, 2026\-09\-15; corrected 2026\-09\-25) → Goals~~ **Savings (no buttons; a tap opens its editor) → Goals (each row's \+) → Income → Recurring Expenses summary, with the threshold note under it → two learn links (Decided 2026\-10\-09, with the owner: structure M3, Goals second)** | habit\-loop | Savings | Savings full editor (§3.6) · Goal editor \+ calculator (§6) · Recurring Expense list and editor (§3.11) | Continuity framing, not a scoreboard — Savings leads because it's the figure the whole model protects |
| Budget → Categories | Total spent → category rows → \+ New | habit\-loop | the no\-red framing itself | Category editor (§3.6) | The screen most tempting to default to status\-board; the tick\+hatch bar (§2.1) is the register correction in action |
| Activity | Savings (same figure, same affordances — §3.6, §3.7) → Accounts (Credit, Loan) → \+ New account **→ Log (Activity log, Import a statement) → *"Accounts are informational."*, which opens the rest of that note in a sheet rather than expanding in place (Decided 2026\-10\-09, with the owner)** | card\-grid | Savings | Savings full editor (§3.6) · Account detail screen, which hosts §9.2's payoff\-outlook panel for Credit · transaction ledger (§7.3, ~~later pass~~ built 2026\-09\-14) | No comparison\-to\-target exists here at all — naturally ambient regardless of overall app tone |
| Learn | intro note → accordion cards, hook\-first | card\-grid | flat, no primary | Loan calculator (§9.1) · Credit purchase calculator (§9.2, Surface 2) · Overtime calculator (§10.2) — all deferred | Reference content sits outside the tone spectrum's tension — flat emphasis avoids implying some facts matter more |
| Reports | **Buffer (default)** · Spending · Income vs Expenses (only if Income enabled, §5.4) · Savings vs. Owed (only if enabled, §5.3) | ambient\-reflection | trend itself, never "this month vs. plan" | — | The purest ambient\-reflection case in the app; Buffer leads per §3.7/§5.1's reasoning. The emphasis note applies to Buffer, Income vs Expenses and Savings vs. Owed; Spending (§5.2) is a single\-month share breakdown by design |
| Settings | Appearance & accessibility → Income → Notifications → Savings vs. Owed toggle (inline) → Other | **hub\-and\-spoke** | the Appearance row | **Appearance & accessibility · Income · Notifications** · Tour (§11.6) · Profile switcher hook (§8.3, ~~inert until §8 is built~~ live since 2026\-09\-14) | Pure configuration, tone\-neutral — and the only screen long by construction rather than by content, which is why it is the one that gets restructured (§4.7) |

**The Recurring Expenses summary shows the current month's scheduled occurrence total (Decided, 2026\-09\-02).** That is the same figure §3.5's threshold tests — §3.11's count/apportionment math for the current calendar month, whether or not the sweep has fired — labelled as this month's total. The section was previously named with no defined content, and the plausible candidates diverge sharply. For a person with rent €900 monthly, a €40 biweekly bill landing twice this month, and a settled €1,200 yearly insurance apportioned at €100: face value reads **€2,140**, this month's scheduled total **€1,080**, the amount actually swept so far **€0 or €1,080** depending on the day, and the next single occurrence **€900**. Showing anything other than the figure §3.5 is silently testing behind the same section would leave the note and the number above it disagreeing on the app's most\-visited screen.

### 4\.3 Budget (Decided)

Segmented control: **Envelopes | Categories** (Activity removed — see §4.1).

**The segmented control is a narrow\-width affordance (Decided, 2026\-09\-24).** It exists because one measure holds one view. Where §4.8's column rule fits **two** columns, both views render side by side — Envelopes left, Categories right, in this control's own order — and the control is **not drawn at all**, rather than drawn inert. Below two columns it is exactly as it always was, which is every phone and every narrow window. The reasoning, including the §2.4 register argument it had to answer, is in §4.8; what belongs here is the consequence: **`sub` selects a view only while one column is showing**, and a window resized from wide to narrow returns to whichever view `sub` last held.

**The Recurring Expenses summary unwraps at two columns (Decided, 2026\-09\-24).** §4.2's Envelopes order keeps Recurring Expenses as a one\-row summary — *"6 recurring expenses · €1,364/mo"* — pushing to the list. At two columns it expands in place to show its occurrences, on the owner's instruction: *"once the columns become two, the collapsed '6 recurring expenses' should unwrap not to leave that much empty space."* The row's total and its push target are unchanged; what changes is that the space the second column frees is spent on the items rather than left blank. This is §4.8's breadth applied to the one section that was already a deliberate collapse.

- **Envelopes**\: see §4.2 row above; full detail in §6 (Goals) and §3 (Savings/Income/Recurring).
- **Categories**\: plan vs. actual for discretionary spending. "Spent" is directly editable (§3.6) and incremented by quick\-add — it is a **stored figure, not one recomputed from the ledger on read**, since §12 makes Transactions log/history only and never the source of truth for the field they touch. Category automation — **ingesting real transaction or bank data from outside the app** to populate it — ~~is **explicitly out of scope for this build and remains genuinely open** (§13.1, item 1): no mechanism is decided.~~ **was resolved 2026\-09\-06 — the next paragraph (struck 2026\-09\-25).** **Narrowed 2026\-09\-02 (prototype cross\-check)** from "deriving `spent` from real transaction or bank data," which read as though internal derivation were unsettled too; it is not, and `sci-fin-prototype.html` recomputing `spent` from its own ledger each render is prototype drift rather than an alternative this document leaves open. ~~Note that §7.3 has already closed off the most obvious route for this app's platforms (SMS/notification scanning), so anything here would need a different approach entirely rather than a revival of that one.~~

**Resolved 2026\-09\-06 — statement import, with an opt\-in reminder (Decided; built).** The person exports a statement from their bank as **CSV or OFX** and opens it in the app; it is parsed **on this device**, and nothing is uploaded anywhere. §8.3's "local, single\-owner data, not an online multi\-tenant system" therefore still describes the app, which is the property that decided this over every alternative that reads better on coverage. **Walked on the device 2026\-09\-23 (§11.5)** through the real picker, with a vetoed row and a reassigned one.

**What confirming an import says (Decided, 2026\-09\-15):** the screen returns to its start with one note — *"12 entries added — €648 out, €240 in."* — and a learn link to the Activity log; `importSummary()` makes the line, the store keeps the count and the two totals in memory only (`importDone`), cleared when the next file is chosen. Before this the only acknowledgement was the "Last import" caption, and the critique found a person unable to tell whether anything had landed.

- **A parsed row is a suggestion, never a write.** Every row lands on a review screen where it can be excluded and its target reassigned, and one Confirm at the end names the total. This is §3.6's Confirm/Cancel discipline met over a reviewed set rather than forty separate prompts — and it is the whole reason the mechanism is admissible. Testing a parser against held\-out statement text showed that a rule list is *reactive*: each new class of misread is discovered by getting it wrong once. Writing straight into `spent` — a stored figure with no ledger to reconcile against (§12) — would make that a corrupted figure. Behind a Confirm it is a suggestion someone declines.
- **Each row carries the statement's own date**, which governs the ledger and every report derived from it while the live figure moves now — §3.6's existing rule, applied to a third entry point rather than a new one.
- **Rows the app already accounts for arrive unticked, with the reason shown.** A payee matching a Recurring Expense is skipped, because §3.11 already swept it and importing it again would double\-count against Savings. So is anything already imported. Both are visible and re\-tickable: the app states what it thinks and lets the person overrule it.
- **Money in goes to Savings, not to a Category** (§3.3, §5.4), ~~and an outflow belonging to no label goes to Savings as §5.2's Uncategorised.~~ **and every outflow needs a target before review confirms (Decided 2026\-10\-09, with §3.6's "nothing leaves untracked"): a Category, *Spent from* a Goal that holds enough, *Charged to* a card, or *Paid off* an Account (which also takes it from Savings, since the statement shows it left). Until each has one, review says how many are still open and its confirm stays off; Goals spent from twice are checked in write order, so two rows that together overdraw one are caught.** A Category is never guessed on first sight — only a rule the person taught the app by assigning that payee before will pre\-fill one.
- **Deduplication is exact where the format allows it and stated where it isn't.** OFX carries `<FITID>`, a per\-account transaction id, so re\-importing an overlapping range is resolved by identity. CSV has no such field, so a synthetic key over date, amount and description stands in. **Both keys are recorded for every row and both are checked**, because a CSV export and an OFX export of one period share no id and the second pass would otherwise re\-import all of it silently. The cost is that two genuinely separate identical purchases on one day collide; that trade is taken because a false duplicate is unticked and visible while a missed one is a silent double\-count.
- **Cash is not special** (§3.2): a withdrawal is an expense like any other, and someone who wants to track the cash separately has §6.3's no\-target Goal — **since 2026\-10\-09 a withdrawal can be put into it and its spending *Spent from* it (§3.6).**
- **The reminder is opt\-in and default off** (§4.7). It asks for an action, so it follows auto\-deposit's precedent (§3.3) rather than the New Month notification's. It opens the picker and does nothing else — no import ever begins on its own.

**What it does not do, stated so it isn't assumed:** nothing is captured between imports, which is the whole of its coverage gap against continuous capture; and a bank that exports neither CSV nor OFX is not served by it. Both were known when it was chosen (§13.1).

### 4\.4 Activity (Decided)

Promoted top\-level tab. Shows the same Savings figure as Envelopes (\+ quick\-add; **since 2026\-10\-09 the floating \+ on a phone and each row's \+ on a desktop, §3.6**), then **Accounts** (Credit, Loan — §7.1) as a single flat list, the Everyday/Tracking distinction having been retired along with Checking/Cash (§13). Full CRUD (add/rename/retype/delete, direct balance edit) plus quick\-add as the lower\-friction path. **Statement import (§4.3) is reached from here too (added 2026\-09\-06)** — it produces Transactions, so it belongs with the money itself rather than in Settings, and the reminder that prompts it is configured under Notifications (§4.7) because that is what it is. The full transaction ledger (browse/filter past Transactions, §7.3) ~~remains a later pass.~~ **was built 2026\-09\-14 (§7.3, `LedgerScreen.kt`; corrected 2026\-09\-25).**

### 4\.5 Learn — the *Science* tab since 2026\-10\-28 (Decided; source mapping and order added 2026\-09\-02)

Accordion cards, each grounded in a cited research finding (§2.6), each with a hook line, an expandable body, and a source line. Flat emphasis — no topic is weighted above another; the list order below is fixed for predictability (§2.5's low\-distraction/autism considerations), and is not a ranking.

**Current topics, each mapped to the §2.6 finding that supplies its source line (added 2026\-09\-02 — previously the topics were named with no stated source, and §14 forbids taking the copy from the prototype):**

1. **Why nothing turns red** — ostrich effect / financial shame, Gladstone et al. (audit §2.3).
2. **Why naming a goal helps** — future self\-continuity, Hershfield (audit §2.4). **This is one card, not two (resolved 2026\-09\-02):** "future self\-continuity" and "why naming a goal helps" were listed separately but cite the same finding — the continuity effect *is* the reason naming works, so splitting them would print the same source line twice and imply two mechanisms where there is one.
3. **Why categories work** — mental accounting, Thaler (audit §2.1, §3.3), with earmarking and partitioning, Soman & Cheema (audit §3.3), as the second half of the body.
4. **Financial well\-being over adherence** — the CFPB Financial Well\-Being framework (audit §3.7).
5. **Credit scores, and what this app doesn't do** — §9.3's own bullets rather than an audit finding, with the CFPB's credit\-invisibility research supplying the source line. **Added 2026\-09\-23, last in the order (Decided with the person):** the four above are findings about how people handle money; this one is a tension the app's own stance creates, so it reads as a coda, and appending it changes nothing in a list this section fixes for predictability.

6. **What your time is worth, and why asking is hard** — §10's topic, built 2026\-10\-23; its sources are the research audit's §2.5, added the same day.
7. **What borrowing really costs** — §9.1's and §9.2's topic, loans and cards together, built 2026\-10\-23 (owner): the monthly payment and the printed minimum hide the whole cost (research audit §2.6, added the same day). It names the app's lean toward saving as a stance reasoned from the findings, says nothing in favour of new borrowing (owner: a line calling borrowing *often the right call* was rejected as misleading), keeps a loan or balance someone already carries neutral as §9.1 does, and carries no figures, leading instead into both calculators. Linked from a loan's screen and from a card's, there under the payoff outlook it explains and above the credit note.
8. **Why money on hand matters** — Reports' Age of Money (§5.1), built 2026\-10\-25 (owner; research audit §2.7): cash on hand goes with feeling secure about money (Ruberton, Gladstone & Lyubomirsky, correlational), and scarcity pulls attention towards itself; the broader *bandwidth* claim is named as not having held up and is not made. The cushion reading is stated as the app's own.
9. **Why uneven months are normal** — Reports' Income vs Expenses (§5.4), built 2026\-10\-25 (owner; research audit §2.8): month\-to\-month swings in income and in spending are common across incomes and don't move together, and spending following pay is partly bills falling due; no smoothing is prescribed. Shown in Learn whatever Income is set to, though its link exists only where the view does.

**The order is the order the links are met in (owner, 2026\-10\-23), replacing the fixed order above:** Budget from the top down, then Categories, then Activity, and so on; topics no screen links to follow, in their existing order. Budget's two links were swapped the same day to read in the order of the sections they speak to (Goals above anything category\-related). That gives **Why naming a goal helps → Why categories work → Why nothing turns red → What borrowing really costs → Credit scores → Why money on hand matters → Why uneven months are normal → Financial well\-being → What your time is worth**; the numbers above stay as the topics' names elsewhere in this document, not their positions. Learn's second column (§4.8) opens on the first.

("Why a specific check\-in day helps" was dropped, 2026\-07\-25 — a stale leftover from the retired check\-in\-day picker; §3.9/§11.4 killed that concept outright.)

~~The body copy itself is still to be written, but it must be written from `sci-fin-research-audit.md` at the sections named above, never from the prototype's card text (§14).~~ **Until it is (Decided, 2026\-09\-14), the built screen carries the four titles and their source lines, and each body reads one line saying the copy is to be written from the audit — the audit file was not in the project folder when the screen was built, so nothing was invented in its place.**

**The route to the copy, revised (Decided, 2026\-09\-24).** The blocker the 2026\-09\-14 note names is gone: `sci-fin-research-audit.md` is in the project folder (renamed from its download name 2026\-09\-25). And the four bodies are not unwritten — they exist in `sci-fin-prototype.html`, complete, with hooks and source lines. The rule above forbade using them. It is **narrowed rather than dropped**: the prototype's bodies are **adopted as the starting text, and each paragraph is checked against the audit section this list names for that topic** — §2.3 for topic 1, §2.4 for topic 2, §2.1 and §3.3 for topic 3, §3.7 for topic 4. Anything the audit does not support is cut or rewritten, and the check is recorded per paragraph rather than asserted for the card. What §14's rule was protecting is the property that every claim traces to a cited finding, and a paragraph checked against a named section has that property whatever hand first wrote it; what it cannot survive is copy adopted *unchecked*, which is not what this decides. **One discrepancy found in the search (2026\-09\-24):** the prototype calls topic 4 *"A perfect month was never the point"* under the id `not-a-perfect-month`, where this list and `LearnScreen.kt` both say *"Financial well\-being over adherence"*. The list here is authoritative and the code already matches it; the prototype is the drifted copy.

**The four bodies are in (2026\-10\-10, owner\-approved).** Checked per paragraph against the audit. Topic 1 (§2.3): ¶1 kept with one narrowing, since the audit supports shame and failure framing driving avoidance, not red as a colour, so "red styling" became "feedback framed as failure"; ¶2 describes §2.1's tick\+hatch bar as built; ¶3 cut to the colour\-blindness point, because "the one non\-colour encoding in the app" and "never hidden" were unverified. Topic 2 (§2.4): ¶1 is the audit's stranger finding; ¶2's "measurably increases saving" was narrowed to "in controlled studies … has measurably raised saving", as the audit states it of interventions; ¶3 describes the goal editor. Topic 3 (§2.1, §3.3): Thaler's windfalls, Soman & Cheema's partitioning and its emergency edge, all as the audit states them. Topic 4 (§3.7): the CFPB definition; the prototype's "the app opens on how long your money has been sitting there" was false (the app opens on Budget) and now names Reports' Buffer. Source lines are the prototype's plain sentences, since "(audit §2.3)" was an internal reference shown to the reader.

~~**Reading material for §4.6's report views is deferred (2026\-09\-24).**~~ **Built 2026\-10\-25 for the two views it named — topics 8 and 9 above, each linked last in its view's supporting column (owner).** Spending keeps topic 1's mapping and Savings vs. Owed its own note. Of the five topics above, only topic 1 (Spending's bars) and topic 4 (the tab's register as a whole) map to a report at all; §5.1's Age of Money and §5.4's Income vs Expenses have none, and inventing them needs findings to ground them. Per the owner: *"Additional reading material will be scoped at a later point, starting from scientific research."* Until then §4.8's supporting column on Reports carries each view's own existing explanatory material.

**Deferred, design resolved, not yet built:** *(corrected 2026\-09\-25: both calculators are built — §13.3; the Loan\-education text and §10's topic and calculator are what remain)* ~~a Loan\-education topic~~ **(built 2026\-10\-23 as topic 7, covering cards too)** and calculator (§9.1), a Credit Card pre\-decision purchase calculator (§9.2, Surface 2), and an Overtime & Self\-Worth topic and calculator (§10) are all planned to join this list. **The Overtime & Self\-Worth topic and calculator were built 2026\-10\-23 (topic 6 above).** ~~a Credit Score education topic with its dated country\-resource list (§9.3),~~ **— built 2026\-09\-23 and now topic 5 above.**

### 4\.6 Reports (Decided; Income vs Expenses made conditional 2026\-09\-02)

**Buffer / Age of Money is the default sub\-view and is always present.** **Spending** is always present. The other two are conditional:

- **Income vs Expenses** appears only when Income is enabled (§5.4, 2026\-09\-02). With Income off there is no income series to draw — `isIncome` is set only by auto\-deposit or the "↓" button (the Savings sentence's *Income* since 2026\-10\-09, §3.6), neither of which exists — so the report would be a permanent flat zero line beside a real expense series: exactly the "hole where income should be" §3.3 forbids. Money that arrives without a paycheck (a birthday gift, a repayment from a friend) is Savings, not income, and the app should not imply otherwise every time someone opens Reports.
- **Savings vs. Owed** appears only once enabled in Settings (§4.7, §5.3, off by default).

**The trend window is the person's to choose (Decided, 2026\-09\-24).** §5.1's Age of Money, §5.3's Savings vs. Owed and §5.4's Income vs Expenses were each computed over a **fixed six months** — `months: Int = 6` in the engine, with every call site taking the default. The figure was never decided, only defaulted, and it is what made §4.8's withdrawn width clause wrong: six points do not become more legible in a wider window, because there is nothing further to resolve. The window is now offered as **3, 6, 12 or 24 months**, defaulting to **6** so nothing changes for a person who never touches it, and stored as `Settings.trendMonths` (§12). The control sits **on the report it governs** rather than in Settings, because the thing it changes is in view while it is used; at §4.8's two columns it sits in the supporting column, and at one column beneath the chart. **Spending (§5.2) carries no control** — it is a single\-month composition with no trend for a window to apply to. **A window longer than the history available draws what exists rather than padding**, on §2.1's rule that an unfillable shape implies a completion condition that does not exist; §5.1's own leading\-empty\-point rule already does this at six and is unchanged by the choice.

So a default Income\-off install sees two sub\-views; a fully configured install sees four. **The control's labels are *Buffer · Spending · Income · Owed* (Decided, 2026\-09\-15):** a quarter of a 320–360dp row holds 71–81px, and "Income vs Expenses" (113px) and "Savings vs. Owed" (131px) fit it at no size, so the earlier fix of dropping four labels to the caption size (2026\-09\-14) is withdrawn in favour of the two short names, which fit at full size on every phone width; each view's full name is its panel's heading. Options considered with the person: the caption\-size labels, a sideways\-scrolling row, wrapping chips — all kept four labels that could not be read at a glance or put the fourth out of sight; renaming was their ask, measured before it was accepted. All use the ambient\-reflection pattern (§2.4): a trend or plain figure is the primary visual, never a color\-coded alert or a "you're behind" state. Savings vs. Owed's dip, when shown, is drawn identically to its rises; Income vs Expenses uses one accent at two opacities, never a red/green pair.

### 4\.7 Settings (Decided; permission note and Profile hook clarified 2026\-09\-02)

**Appearance & accessibility** (§2.5, includes **Currency**, §11.1 — the onboarding flow's landing point, default **€**) → **Income** (the off\-by\-default toggle, §3.3, with the payday date field(s) and the auto\-deposit toggle) → **Notifications** → **Savings vs. Owed** (off by default, §5.3 — a single toggle, since enabling it is the only decision it requires) → **Other**.

**A hub of rows, not a flat column (Decided, 2026\-09\-03; Appearance pushed to a spoke 2026\-09\-04).** The order above is unchanged; what changed is depth. As a single column this screen measured **1,894px against a 663px viewport — 2.9 screens at the default text scale and 4.0 at xl** — and it is the only screen in the app that is long *by construction*: every person gets all of it whatever their data, so no amount of content design shortens it. It is also the safest screen in the app to restructure, since a settings list carries no motivational register at all (§2.4's single\-column is tone\-neutral), so changing its shape cannot change how the app reads.

**Appearance & accessibility, Income and Notifications each become their own screen**, reached from rows carrying their current state — *"~~Slate~~ Swiss Blue · Light →"*, *"On · every 2 weeks →"*, *"New Month on →"* — so the hub still answers "what is this set to" without being opened. **Savings vs. Owed stays inline**, because this section already calls it "a single toggle, since enabling it is the only decision it requires": putting one switch behind a tap costs more than it saves.

**Appearance was left inline for one pass and then pushed (2026\-09\-04).** The argument for keeping it up front was §11.1's Currency landing and §11.6's stop 8, both of which point at that panel. It did not survive measurement: the panel is **658px on its own — half the hub** — and was the single reason the hub still ran to two screens. Both callers are served by navigating *into* the sub\-screen rather than by keeping 658px one level up, which is what §11.1 step 4 and §11.6 stop 8 now specify.

| | flat column | hub, Appearance inline | hub, Appearance a spoke |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Settings hub, default text scale | 1,894px (2.9 screens) | 1,288px (1.9) | **708px (1.07)** |
| Settings hub, text scale xl | 2,663px (4.0 screens) | 1,686px (2.5) | **858px (1.29)** |
| deepest screen under Settings, default | 2.9 | 1.9 | **1.17** — Appearance 774px, hub 708px, Income and Notifications both fit |
| deepest screen under Settings, xl | 4.0 | 2.5 | **1.52** — the Appearance spoke, 1,007px |

Measured with the reviewer\-only "View the fresh\-install flow" button still in the hub; without it the shipping hub is **626px at the default scale, inside a single viewport**, and 754px at xl.

**The OS\-permission line is now implemented** on the Notifications screen rather than merely specified — `notificationPermissionGranted` (§12) previously existed as a field nothing read.

- **Notifications** carries the New Month toggle (§3.9), default **on** — the one notification toggle default\-on rather than off, since it carries no real content and nothing to consent away from beyond a single gentle ping. Per\-expense due\-date reminders (§3.11) are configured on each Recurring Expense rather than here, since their lead time is per\-item, but they ride the same scheduling subsystem and the same OS permission. **A third toggle arrived 2026\-09\-06: the import reminder (§4.3), default off.** It rides the same subsystem again — which is why it cost almost nothing to add — and it is default *off* rather than on because it asks for an action, following auto\-deposit's precedent (§3.3) rather than New Month's. **When the OS\-level permission is denied or switched off, a plain line sits beneath the toggle (added 2026\-09\-02):** *"Notifications are turned off for Sci\-Fin in your system settings — New Month and due\-date reminders won't appear."* Same `feasibility-note` register as everywhere else (§2.3) — a fact, not a warning, with no prompt to go and change it. This is the one surface that reads `notificationPermissionGranted` (§12), which previously existed as a stored field nothing consumed: §11.5 correctly says no special *handling* is needed, but a toggle that displays as on while nothing can ever fire is the app misreporting its own state, which §2.3's autonomy stance rules out. The value is re\-read from the OS on app open rather than trusted from storage, since the permission can be revoked at any time.
- **Other** carries: ~~data & backup (placeholder)~~ **Data & backup (Decided and built 2026\-09\-14 — specified below)**, ~~security & lock (placeholder; hidden from the hub since 2026\-10\-10)~~ **Security & lock (Decided and built 2026\-10\-20 — specified below)**, ~~**replay onboarding** — a real, defined feature rather than a placeholder: a condensed static summary of the tutorial plus a button to replay the full live\-overlay tour (§11.6), resetting no data either way~~ **the Tour (2026\-10\-27, §11.6)** — an index of the tour's stops opened under its row, each a link to that stop's screen with its window, and the slideshow to replay, resetting no data either way — and the **avatar/Profile switcher hook** (§8.3). **The hook lives here, and nowhere else (Decided, 2026\-09\-02):** §8.3 previously placed it on Budget's top row while this section and §11.6 placed it in Settings, so an implementer had three sections giving two locations. Settings wins because two Decided sections already put it there and §8.4 defers Profiles entirely — a switcher used rarely, and inert until §8 is built, doesn't warrant top\-level real estate. §8.3 has been corrected to match.

~~The two placeholders above are genuinely unbuilt and undesigned; they are logged in §13.2 rather than left to read as though they were specified.~~ ~~One placeholder remains — security & lock — and it stays logged in §13.2. Its row is hidden from the hub rather than shown as a placeholder, and returns when app lock exists (owner’s decision 2026\-10\-10).~~ **No placeholder remains: Security & lock was designed and built 2026\-10\-20 (below), and its row is back on the hub.**

**Data & backup (Decided and built 2026\-09\-14).** A spoke off the hub like Notifications, holding one action each way. Harvested from three sibling projects that converged on the same shape independently; the one thing none of them had to decide is the one thing Sci\-Fin does: the file holds **every Profile**, because §8.4's wrap point means "everything on this device" is the Profiles array and nothing above it.

- **Export** writes one JSON file — `app`, `schemaVersion` (§12), `exportedAt`, `activeProfileId`, and `profiles[]` each carrying `id`, `name` and the full wrapped data (§8.4, ImportState included). It goes wherever the person puts it; the app keeps only the date of the last export, for the hub row. Nothing is sent anywhere (§1's registry).
- **Restore is a replace, not a merge, and says so before it happens.** The file is parsed and **validated whole before anything changes**; a valid file lands on a review card naming its date, its profile count and names, and its transaction count, with a plain note that what is on the device now will be gone, and a Confirm/Cancel pair (§3.6's discipline). Merging was considered and rejected: two Profiles with the same id from two devices have no correct merge, and a merge that guesses is the phantom\-duplicate class of bug §3.9 and §4.3 each already had to design out. **Walked on the device 2026\-09\-23 (§11.5):** the card's figures were the file's own, and after Confirm every count returned.
- **Validation returns nothing, and then nothing is imported.** A file is refused whole when it is not an object, has no profiles, has a profile lacking id/name/data, has any of the five entity lists present but not a list, or has an unreadable Savings figure. A field that is simply *absent* takes its default rather than refusing the file — an absent key cannot have held data, so refusing it would protect nothing; a field present in the wrong shape is damage, and does. A file whose `schemaVersion` is **higher than the app's is refused whole** with the two numbers shown (§12's storage rule) — the failure a version exists to prevent is a silent partial load that looks like success. A file with no version reads as 1.
- **Two things are repaired rather than refused,** on the principle that a malformed cosmetic is no reason to refuse someone's money: an unknown theme becomes ~~Slate~~ Swiss Blue, and a retired theme's token its nearest new theme (2026\-10\-07) and an unknown mode becomes System, because those two go straight onto the shell and only §2.5's declared combinations carry tokens. Absent fields, ImportState's included, take their defaults.
- **Order of the write is stated for the native store:** write everything, then load, so a process killed between the two leaves the old data or the new and never neither.
- **A password on the file (Decided 2026\-10\-19/20 with the owner; built 2026\-10\-20).** Export asks whether to protect the file: **optional, and required while the app PIN is on**, so a file is never easier to read than the app. A password is at least 8 characters, typed twice, and **always comes with a hint** (every file password; the PIN's hint stays optional), which may not be the password itself. The plain file is wrapped whole in an AES\-256\-GCM envelope keyed by PBKDF2\-HMAC\-SHA256 (600,000 iterations; a file asking for fewer than 100,000 or more than 5,000,000 is refused). Readable outside the encryption: that it is Sci\-Fin's, the date and the hint, all three bound to the ciphertext so none can be altered. Restore recognises such a file and asks for its password first, with the hint on request, before §4.7's usual review; a wrong password says so and changes nothing. A lost password cannot be recovered, and the export step says so.
- **Automatic copy (Decided 2026\-10\-19/20 with the owner, after the mockups; built 2026\-10\-20).** On Data & backup: how often — **Off, Daily or Weekly** — and a folder the person picks, into which the whole backup is written as `sci-fin-copy-<date>.json`, so a sync app the person already runs can carry it off the device. **The newest 14 are kept**; older copies of the app's own are deleted, and nothing else in the folder is touched. Turning it on asks for the folder first. The copy runs from each platform's existing daily background task (Android's top\-up worker, Windows' scheduled `--sync-notifications` run) and on every open of the app, so a missed day is caught up; Copy now writes one at once. Its schedule, folder and password are device\-only (`copy_schedule`, schema 7), never in a backup.
  - **A password for the copies** follows the file rules above (8 characters, a required hint) and is optional — **required while the app PIN is on**. It is kept sealed by the platform's key store (Android Keystore; Windows DPAPI for the current user), so the copy can run with nobody there to type it. Turning the PIN on while the copies have no password **pauses them and asks for one at once**; "Later" leaves them paused and the page says so. A hand export offers the copies' password first, "A different password" second.
  - **A failure** (folder gone, access withdrawn, disk full, a password the key store can no longer read) is recorded on the page with the last good copy, and **said once in a notification per run of failures**; the next success clears it.
- **What it is not:** sync. There is no continuous feed, no folder watched, no second device known to the app. A person who wants two devices to agree exports on one and restores on the other, and the review card is what stops that from being a surprise.

**Security & lock (Decided 2026\-10\-19 with the owner, after the mockups; built 2026\-10\-20).** A spoke off the hub, after Data & backup. Of the four shapes mocked — none, the device's own prompt, a PIN of the app's own, the device prompt with Windows Hello — the owner chose **the app's own PIN, optional and off by default**, because it is the only one that works the same on the phone and the desktop.

- **The PIN** is 4 to 6 digits, typed twice, with an **optional hint** the person writes, which may never be the PIN itself (compared trimmed and ignoring case). It is stored only as a salted PBKDF2\-HMAC\-SHA256 hash (120,000 iterations, the cost kept beside it so it can be raised) in its own table, `app_lock` (§12, schema 6). **It is device\-only:** it is never in a backup file, and a restore, which rewrites `app_state`, leaves it alone.
- **The PIN guards the screen, not the data.** The store is not encrypted with it, so a forgotten PIN can only cost what a backup cannot bring back, and the sandbox, not the hash, is what keeps the file from being copied.
- **When it locks:** every open of the app, and on returning after **"Lock after leaving the app": Immediately, after 1 minute (the default) or after 5 minutes.** Leaving is the app going out of sight on Android (another app, Home, the screen off — not a notification shade or a dialog over it) and the window losing focus on Windows (another window, or minimised). The app's own file pickers are not leaving. "Lock now" locks at once. Times are kept on the monotonic clock, not the wall clock.
- **The gate is drawn instead of the app, never over it,** so nothing beneath can be read or reached by a screen reader. Back on the gate is the system's. The field tries itself on the last digit, so no second tap is needed. "Show hint" reveals the hint on request.
- **Wrong PINs:** the first four cost nothing; from the fifth, a wait of 30 seconds that doubles with each further miss, never more than an hour. The count is stored, so restarting the app owes the wait again. A wrong PIN typed to turn the lock off counts the same — turning it off asks for the PIN, so a phone left unlocked cannot have its lock removed. **Change PIN** (added 2026\-10\-20) asks for the current PIN the same way, keeps the "lock after" choice and starts the hint as the one kept; typing the same PIN as the new one changes only the hint.
- **A forgotten PIN — "Forgot PIN?" on the gate — erases everything on this device and starts over** (chosen over "reinstall only", 2026\-10\-19: on Windows an uninstall leaves the data folder under `%LOCALAPPDATA%` behind, so reinstalling would have opened on the same gate). A warning names the last export date; the person types ERASE; every Profile, the app row, the lock and §12's kept snapshots are deleted, the file is vacuumed so the rows' pages go too, the OS schedule is cleared, and the app opens on §11.1's screen 1, which offers a restore. Erasing reveals nothing, so it opens no door the lock closes.
- **On Android, while a PIN is set, the window is marked secure:** the recent\-apps preview is blank and screenshots of Sci\-Fin are refused.

The in\-prototype "Preview a budget notification" button is a **reviewer aid only** and will be removed from the finished product.

### 4\.8 Desktop layout: adaptive reflow, with the transform specified (Decided, 2026\-09\-02; built 2026\-09\-04; **reopened 2026\-09\-23**)

**The opening size is 1100 × 760 outer, clamped to the screen's work area (Decided 2026\-09\-26, with the owner — closes §13.1 item 6).** Outer is what AWT sets, so the content is 1084 × 721; on a smaller screen the window shrinks to the work area (`openingSize` in the desktop host) rather than opening under the taskbar — 720 tall on a 1366 × 768 laptop, 672 on 1920 × 1080 at 150%. The window's size changes no component's size, since §2.5's screen factor is 1 above 600dp; it changes only the margin and how much shows before scrolling.

> **Reopened 2026\-09\-23, at the person's direction.** Asked which width the scale factor should be proportional to on Windows, the answer was that *the desktop app will not be a copy of the phone app, so these decisions will not be taken once for both.* This section decided the opposite — adaptive reflow over a separate desktop shell — and decided it on register grounds rather than on cost, so the reasoning below is not withdrawn and is not to be discarded quietly: it is the case the replacement has to answer. What is now open is the conclusion, not the argument. Until it is settled, §2.5's screen factor is specified for the phone only, and the desktop frame is left as built — including the two\-pane defect found on 2026\-09\-15, which is deliberately not repaired while the frame it belongs to may not survive. **It was retired with rule 2 on 2026\-09\-24 (note added 2026\-09\-25).**

§1 commits to Windows 10/11 as a first\-class target, and until now no section said what the app looks like there — §4.1's five bottom tabs are a phone pattern and `sci-fin-prototype.html` is a fixed 360×756 phone frame. The choice was between reflowing the phone layout adaptively and authoring a separate desktop shell. **Adaptive reflow wins, and not primarily on cost.**

**Why not a separate desktop shell.** What authoring one actually buys is density: use the width to show more at once — Envelopes and Categories side by side rather than behind §4.3's segmented control, Activity as a dashboard grid, every figure visible simultaneously. That payoff is one this app is designed not to want. §4.2 assigns Budget the **habit\-loop** pattern and warns in its own words that Categories is "the screen most tempting to default to status\-board," with §2.1's tick\+hatch bar as the register correction in action. A desktop IA that surfaces every figure at once converts the app's most\-visited screen into exactly the scoreboard §2.2 and §2.4 exist to prevent. §4.2's section orders were derived from the register audit — they are psychological, not spatial, so nothing about a wide window makes a different order better. What is left of the shell's case is then pure cost: two authored layouts, and §2.5's eight palettes, eleven tokens, four text scales *(now the OS's one; removed from the app 2026\-09\-25, schema 5)* and two densities verified twice.

**Why reflow needed specifying anyway.** The shell's one real advantage was that it forced an answer to "what do five bottom tabs become on a desktop." Reflow left as a bare width breakpoint never answers that, which is why it was logged as a genuine tie rather than an obvious call. The answer is given here instead. **The contested question was never reflow\-versus\-shell; it was specified\-versus\-unspecified.**

**The column rule (Decided, 2026\-09\-24).** The desktop does not reflow into panes. It lays out **columns of one measure**, and the window decides how many fit:

> A column is **one measure wide and never wider**. The content area holds `N` columns where `N` is the largest count satisfying `N × measure + (N − 1) × gap ≤ content`. Surplus width is left empty and the block of columns is **centred**. Nothing stretches at any width.

**The measure follows the device's text size (Decided 2026\-09\-26, with the owner — closes §13.1 item 2).** It is 52 characters *of body*, and body is the device's text size, so `measureFor` multiplies the 458dp by the platform's font scale: a line holds about 52 characters at every size. Measured before the change on the real layout: 53 characters at 100%, 44 at 115%, 33 at 150% and 26 at 200%, below the 45–75 reading band from Android's first step up. The consequence the item asked about is accepted: a window too narrow for two such columns reflows to one — a 1280dp tablet at 130% — which is WCAG 1.4.10's norm. It binds only at 600dp and wider; on a portrait phone the screen is narrower than the measure anyway.

**The measure is already in the build and is not a new constant.** `Controls.kt` defines `measureCap` as `base × 0.55 × 52` — 52 characters, **458dp at the default text size** — derived from §2.5's own type ramp rather than borrowed from another application. It is applied to prose today; the rule applies it to the column. Because it is a *maximum*, it is a no\-op on a phone: 360dp less §2.5's padding is 332dp of content, already under it, so this rule changes nothing below 600dp and needs no width\-class branch to say so.

**Why columns rather than panes.** The owner's statement of it: *"the elements should grow until becoming two columns, then they should stay centered"*, and the reason: *"the window changes how crowded the screen gets — keeping the correct amount of density without overstretching the visual elements, which would undermine the meaning of the filling bars."* §2.1's bar is the case that proves it: `Progress.kt` draws it `fillMaxWidth()` at a fixed `height(5.dp)`, so a container that stretches does not scale the bar, it changes its aspect ratio without limit. The column rule bounds that by construction.

**What it produces at the sizes that matter.** With the measure at 458 and §2.5's `gap`, two columns need ~~932~~ **951** of content (measured on the real layout 2026\-09\-26: the page's own padding counts, 2 × 458 \+ 8 \+ 2 × 14). A 1100×760 window gives 1084×721 of client area (§13.1 item 6's window note), and 1084 less the 88dp rail is 996 (both corrected 2026\-09\-25) — so **two columns are the default**, not something the window has to be dragged to reach. The second column is what fills the space rule 2 left empty. **The principle of what it holds is settled in the three paragraphs below (2026\-09\-24); the contents for Activity, Learn and Settings ~~are still §13.1 item 3~~ were decided 2026\-09\-26 — see *The second column on Activity, Learn and Settings*, below** (wording corrected 2026\-09\-25 — it had read as settling both). An earlier draft of this paragraph said Budget's second column is Categories beside Envelopes and called that "exactly what this section said the width should buy". **That misread *Why not a separate desktop shell*, above**, which names Envelopes\-beside\-Categories as the density payoff **this app is designed not to want** — on §4.2's habit\-loop assignment for Budget, and §2.2 and §2.4's case against a scoreboard. The reopening note at the head of this section keeps that argument live: *what is now open is the conclusion, not the argument*. So a proposal to retire §4.3's segmented control on the desktop has to **answer** that reasoning, and cannot cite it as licence.

**What the desktop is for (Decided, 2026\-09\-24).** This section chose reflow over a separate shell, specified the transform, and named its exceptions — without ever stating what the Windows build *exists to do*. That absence is why it could only reason defensively about density: with no purpose to weigh breadth against, every gain in breadth reads as pure register risk. The owner's statement, recorded here as the premise the rest of this section now rests on:

> **The desktop app is a way to log data faster. It is optional. And it should offer a broader view of the information than the phone app.**

The phone remains the complete app — nobody needs a desktop to use Sci\-Fin, and no feature lives only on Windows. What the desktop adds is **speed of entry and breadth of view**, which is consistent with the 2026\-09\-23 reopening's instruction that neither platform is a copy of the other.

**The second column holds another view of the same destination — never another destination (Decided, 2026\-09\-24).** This is the line that keeps breadth from becoming a scoreboard, and it is the owner's own: *"The risk of a change of register is why I didn't propose Activity to become the second column next to Budget."* Two views of one destination leave that destination's job intact — Budget is still Budget, doing Budget's work, with more of it visible at once. Two **destinations** side by side would be a different screen entirely: one that exists to show standing across the whole app rather than to do one thing, which is the status\-board §2.4 forbids without exception. So §4.1's five destinations stay mutually exclusive at every width, and §4.8's columns are always *within* one of them.

**The second column on Activity, Learn and Settings (Decided 2026\-09\-26, with the owner — closes §13.1 item 3).** When two measure\-wide columns fit, the destination's root stays on the left and **the screens it would push open in the second column instead of covering it**; a row opens its screen there, replacing what the column showed rather than stacking on it, and Back returns the column to rest. At rest, **Activity shows the activity log** (§7.3) — entries land beside the accounts as they are logged, which is the desktop's *log faster* purpose; **Learn shows a reading pane**, the chosen topic's text in place of the in\-place accordion, resting on the first topic; and **Settings shows the selected page**, resting on Appearance. The resting view has no Back; the chosen row carries the rail's 12% accent wash, so no new visual was added. On a phone, and in a window too narrow for two columns, nothing changes. **How it was decided:** every candidate view was counted on the real composables over the parity fixture, calibrated against the 2026\-09\-24 census (Spending 12 figures and 11 bars, Income 2 and 12, both reproduced), and every one passed *Why not a second report panel*'s criterion — the densest, Activity with its log, renders 17 figures and 0 bars against Budget's 19 and 6, and none ranks rows. `app-register-layout`'s engine scored every candidate identically (0.858), since it sees section names rather than content, so the choice was the owner's, made from renders of the real screens (`design/mockups-2026-09-26/item3-*.png`).

**Answering *Why not a separate desktop shell* (Decided, 2026\-09\-24).** That paragraph, above, names Envelopes\-beside\-Categories as a density payoff "this app is designed not to want", and §4.8's reopening requires the conclusion's replacement to answer the argument rather than discard it. It is answered on four points, and the conclusion is reversed **for the within\-destination case only**:

1. **No new element appears.** Both views already exist in the app and are reached by one tap. Side by side they carry the same figures, the same §2.1 tick\+hatch encoding and the same captions. §2.4 forbids "any pattern that **scores, ranks, or renders a figure against a target as a verdict**" — co\-visibility does none of those three, and no rule in §2.1, §2.2 or §2.4 names co\-visibility at all. The step from *both visible* to *scoreboard* was this section's own inference, not an inherited rule. *(2026\-09\-25: this point no longer stands alone — both destinations are now judged by the one count under* Why not a second report panel *below.)*
2. **There is no ranking, because there are no peers.** §2.4's prohibition on ordering peers "by any measure of how well they are doing" needs comparable items. Categories already shows every category bar in one list today, so co\-visible reference bars are not the new thing; what the second column adds is *cross\-kind* visibility — Savings and Goals beside Categories — and a goal at 60% does not compare to a category at 80% of plan in any meaningful direction.
3. **Categories is load\-bearing and cannot be reduced further.** The owner's statement: *"Categories is absolutely necessary for the function of the app, there's no hiding it forever or changing the screen to reduce judgement further, it would hinder its purpose."* §4.2 already names it "the screen most tempting to default to status\-board" **and** names the correction already applied — §2.1's tick and hatch, at the row where judgement would otherwise land. The register work on this screen is done in the encoding, not in the visibility.
4. **Breadth is the platform's purpose.** ~~With the paragraph above now stating what the desktop is for, an argument that a broader view is undesirable is an argument against the platform.~~ **The owner's priority, stated as a priority rather than as a refutation (2026\-09\-25, with the owner):** a broader view of the same destination is wanted on the desktop, and its register risk is accepted and watched, not argued away — the audit found the struck sentence adopting the conclusion as its premise. The owner's framing of the residual risk is kept rather than resolved away: *"If it becomes a change of register, I don't know what the solution could be, but it shouldn't limit the chance for the user to have a clearer view of some data."*

**What survives of the original argument** is its scope, and it is now rule 4 above: the rejected thing was a desktop IA surfacing *every figure in the app* at once — "Activity as a dashboard grid, every figure visible simultaneously". That is still rejected, and the within\-destination line is what rejects it.

**The surveyed evidence.** Four desktop applications were driven through a two\-axis resize sweep on 2026\-09\-24 (`design/desktop-prior-art-2026-09-23.md`, its section 15): **none of the four scales its navigation chrome with the window**, all four are exactly reversible, and each holds density by a different one of three mechanisms — hold a measure and leave the surplus (Notion), divide the remainder (Obsidian), centre to a maximum measure (VS Code). A fifth behaviour, collapsing the navigation to protect a content floor (Todoist, at 280dp → 66dp when content would fall below ~484dp), is the form §4.8's own width classes should eventually take: **a protected content minimum, not a window breakpoint.**

**The three rules.**

1. **The navigation container swaps by width class; the destinations never change.** Bottom bar below 600dp, navigation rail at 600dp and above. The same five destinations in §4.1's order, the same selected state, the same badge and empty\-state behaviour. §4.1 is unchanged by this — only its container is.
2. ~~**§4.2's "Pushes to" column is the pane contract.** At 840dp and above, any screen with push targets renders list\-detail in two panes: the list stays in the left pane and what was a pushed detail or editor screen renders in the right. Below 840dp it stays a push, exactly as on a phone. This introduces no new decisions — §4.2 already names every target for every screen, and every one of those screens is a scrolling list that pushes to a detail, which is list\-detail by construction.~~ **Withdrawn (2026\-09\-24), and replaced by the column rule below.** Rule 2 was built as a *fixed* 368dp left pane beside a `weight(1f)` right pane, and a tab root has nothing selected, so the right pane renders the words "Nothing selected." and nothing else. Rendered from the real composables at 2026\-09\-24: on **four of the five destinations** — Budget, Activity, Learn and Settings — that is **627dp empty of a 1084dp window, and 1447dp of a 1904dp one, 76% of the screen**. The list pane never grows, so the wider the window the emptier the app. What rule 2 produced is a void, which no reading of this section wanted. What should occupy that space instead is a separate question, open per destination, and *Why not a separate desktop shell* above constrains it rather than licensing it.
3. **Bottom sheets become centred dialogs at 600dp and above.** Quick\-add (§3.6), the delete confirmations, and both deletion\-reversal prompts (§3.6, §3.11).

**Two per\-screen exceptions, named here rather than discovered later.**

- **Reports never goes two\-pane.** It is the one row in §4.2 whose *Pushes to* cell is "—", so there is nothing to put in a second pane. ~~At expanded width it is a single pane and its charts use the width — the one place in the app where extra space genuinely helps, since §5.1's Buffer trend and §5.4's two series are more legible wide than tall.~~ **The width clause is withdrawn (2026\-09\-24), on measurement.** It claimed the charts are "more legible wide than tall". Legibility bought by width requires resolution to reveal, and there is none to reveal: `Reports.ageOfMoneyTrend`, `Reports.savingsVsOwedTrend` and `Reports.incomeVsExpenses` each take `months: Int = 6`, and **every call site uses the default**, so every chart in this tab is six fixed monthly points. Rendered at one measure they are already fully legible — six labels, six bar pairs, no crowding. Widening to 1815dp would put roughly 300dp between six points: more gaps, not more information. The clause was reasoning about charts in general rather than about this app's data. **Reports is therefore governed by the column rule like every other destination**, and this exception now covers only its first sentence.

- **Reports' second column holds reading material, not a second report (Decided, 2026\-09\-24).** Withdrawing the width clause leaves the tab using about 456 × 530 of a 1904 × 1000 window, which is the same emptiness rule 2 produced in a different form. What fills it was scored rather than argued: six candidates against `designkit`'s own `register_fit` plus co\-visibility counts measured from the real composables, swept across all 1771 weightings on a 0\.05 grid. **Supporting material beside the chart — the base catalog's `two-column` pattern, "primary content beside supporting material" — wins 83\.1% of weightings**, and 85\.3% with buildability excluded. Two results are recorded against interest: `register_fit` mildly *prefers* one column (0\.895 against 0\.775), because `ambient-reflection` sits exactly in §2.4's 0–0\.35 band and scores a full 1\.0 while `two-column` is tone\-neutral at 0\.70 — the second column wins on width used, not on register; and the two candidates that differ only in *what* the supporting material is scored identically on every dimension, so the scoring settles the shape and cannot settle the contents.

  **Why not a second report panel.** The owner's constraint, and the census that confirms it: *"Reports is 4 screens, each carries a degree of judgemental tone, putting them side by side would create a worse feeling than Budget."* Counted from the real composables over the parity fixture on 2026\-09\-24 — Buffer renders 1 figure and 0 bars; **Spending renders 12 figures, 11 filled bars and 11 rows ordered by share**, the only ranked rows anywhere in the app; Income 2 figures and 12 bars; Owed 1 figure and 0 bars. Spending alone is denser than Budget's two columns together, and Spending beside Income would put 14 figures and 23 bars on screen at once. That is the §4.8 within\-destination line reaching its limit: two views of one destination are admissible, but §2.4's prohibition is on what a screen *does*, and four ambient\-reflection readings at once is a status board assembled from calm parts. **One criterion for both destinations (Decided, 2026\-09\-25, with the owner — the audit found Budget and Reports ruled by different tests):** a second column is admissible when the two views together render **no ranked rows** and **no more figures and bars than Budget's own two columns** — the density the owner accepted in deciding Budget's case, used as the calibration rather than a number asserted here. Budget passes by construction; Spending fails on its ranked rows alone, and every report pair fails on the count. Both rulings now follow from one census, rather than from *co\-visibility is never the issue* on one side and *a status board* on the other.
- **Text carries a measure cap.** Learn's accordion bodies (§4.5), every editor's copy, and §2.3's `feasibility-note` treatment are capped at a readable line length rather than running the full width of the window. A paragraph set 1800px wide would cost §2.4's calm register as surely as a scoreboard would.

**What does not change with width:** every figure, every caption, §2.1's tick\+hatch encoding, §2.3's non\-blocking copy, and §2.5's ~~five~~ four accessibility settings (text size is the OS's, removed from the app 2026\-09\-25, schema 5), whose touch and click targets already hold at the platform minimum (48dp Android, 40px Windows) at every density and text scale. A width class changes containers and panes; it never changes what the app says or what it will let a person do.

**Built 2026\-09\-04, with one addition the build forced.** All three rules and both exceptions are implemented. The addition: **Learn joins Reports as single\-pane for now.** §4.2 gives Learn push targets, but all of them are §9/§10's deferred calculators, so honouring rule 2 today would pin a permanently empty second pane beside it. ~~It becomes list\-detail by itself the moment one of those ships — the rule is unchanged, only the set of screens it currently applies to.~~ **Rule 2 was withdrawn 2026\-09\-24 (struck 2026\-09\-25).** The width class is resolved from the app frame's own measured width rather than the viewport's, which is the same answer on a real desktop and the only workable one where the app is embedded. **The measure cap is 52ch, measured rather than guessed:** `ch` is the width of a zero, narrower than this face's average glyph, so an initial 62ch delivered 84 real characters per line — past the 45–75 that is readable. Prose now measures 44 characters at 360dp and 71 at 1,200px, against 175 before the cap existed.

**One assumption stated openly.** Rule 2 assumes list\-detail suits every pushing screen, checked against §4.2's table as it stands. §7.3's transaction ledger is not built yet and is therefore the one screen this rule is written ahead of — though as a browse/filter list over dated rows it is the strongest two\-pane case in the app, so it is expected to fit rather than to force an exception.

## 5\. Reports, in detail

### 5\.1 Buffer / Age of Money — the default view (Decided; engine fully specified 2026\-09\-02)

Shows the person's own trend (e.g., "32 days, up from 18 six months ago") — never a graded verdict ("Excellent\!" / "Keep budgeting").

**The headline is a live figure, computed from the current instant (Decided, 2026\-09\-02).** An earlier sentence described it as "retrospective — how the person has actually behaved over recent months, not a live formula recomputed from the current instant," which the FIFO definition added on 2026\-07\-25 directly contradicts. That sentence is struck: the FIFO bullets are newer and more specific, and "how long the oldest euro currently sitting in Savings has been there" is the honest reading. **The consequence, stated rather than discovered:** the figure rises during inactivity — €150 sitting untouched reads 16 days on the 21st and 26 days on the 31st. That is correct. Money that sits *is* getting older, and a Buffer that only moved when the person did would be measuring activity, not cushion.

**Calculation (Decided, 2026\-07\-25; executable form 2026\-09\-02).** Age of Money is computed via FIFO lot\-consumption — the same underlying idea as the metric popularized by budgeting tools like YNAB — but generalized to work identically whether or not Income is enabled, since §3.3's whole premise is that a Savings\-only user's experience should never be second\-class, and a formula that only understood income deposits would quietly break that promise.

The engine reads the ledger through §3.6's sign table rather than through prose categories, which is the only form it can actually execute:

- **A Transaction's effect on Savings is `+amount` for `key: savings`, `−amount` for `goal:`, `cat:` and `rec:`, and zero for `acct:`.** `acct:` rows are skipped entirely. This replaces an earlier enumeration of lot sources by narrative type, which the engine cannot evaluate — it sees a `key` and a signed `amount` — and which had also fallen out of date, omitting the Savings full editor added three days after it was written.
- **A positive Savings effect opens a dated lot.** This includes Savings' opening value (below), auto\-deposits, general "\+" and Income "↓" quick\-adds (since 2026\-10\-09 the Savings sentence's *Add* and *Income*, §3.6), a direct edit that returns money to Savings, a Goal deletion's accepted reversal (§3.6), a Recurring Expense deletion's accepted reversal (§3.11), and the Savings full editor's positive delta.
- **A negative Savings effect consumes the oldest unconsumed lots first, FIFO.**
- **Savings' opening lot is the first *positive* value `Savings.amount` takes** — by the full editor, a quick\-add, or any action that ends the `null` state (§3.1) — written as a Transaction like any other and dated to that moment. **A €0 starting figure opens no lot**, since a lot is a dated parcel of money and zero has nothing to date; until real money arrives the figure is floored at 0 by the Savings ≤ 0 rule anyway. Without an opening lot the engine has nothing to date at all and reads 0 days for a person with a €9,000 cushion.
- **Ordering:** by `date`, with `loggedAt` as the tie\-break for same\-date rows, so a replay is deterministic.

**The invariant: the sum of unconsumed lots equals `Savings.amount` at every step of the walk (Decided, 2026\-09\-02).** This is what makes the figure mean what the section says it means, and it settles the negative\-Savings case §3.1 explicitly permits. When a decrease exceeds every remaining lot, the excess is **carried as a deficit that the next deposit absorbs before that deposit becomes a datable lot** — it is not dropped. Dropping it lets the lot pool drift permanently out of step with Savings: a €250 overshoot followed by a €1,000 deposit and a €400 sweep leaves lots totalling €600 against a real balance of €350, and every reading from then on describes money that isn't there. **The figure floors at 0 when Savings ≤ 0** — with every lot consumed and then some, there is no oldest euro to date — and shows nothing at all while `Savings.amount` is `null` (§3.1). **The on\-screen note names the Profile's own money (Decided, 2026\-09\-25, with the owner):** *the oldest euro*, *dollar* or *pound*, and *the oldest money* for any other currency — it had said *euro* whatever the currency.

**Goal contributions consume lots like any other outflow, and that is deliberate (§3.2).** Money moved into a Goal has left the unallocated pool, so Buffer falls when a Goal is funded. Per §3.2's reasoning this is the honest reading rather than a defect: the cushion genuinely did shrink, because that money now has a purpose.

**A mistake and its correction are two real movements, not one non\-event.** A €600 quick\-add that consumes the oldest lots, corrected an hour later by a €600 direct edit, opens a *new* lot dated today — so Age of Money falls and does not spring back. The repair is §7.3's pair\-deletion, and only that: **rows removed by pair\-deletion are excluded from the FIFO replay**, which restores the consumed lots exactly, because those two entries describe something that never happened. Outside pair\-deletion's eligibility window the distortion stands, and correctly so — money that genuinely left and genuinely came back three days later is new money in the pool by the only definition this metric uses. This does mean a pair\-deletion changes historical trend points already displayed; that is the feature working, not a side effect.

**The trend line (specified 2026\-09\-02):** ~~**six monthly points**, matching the window §5.3 fixes for the app's other trend report,~~ **`trendMonths` monthly points (§4.6: 3, 6, 12 or 24, default 6; corrected 2026\-09\-25)**, each computed by replaying the same FIFO walk **as of the final instant of that calendar month**, with the current month's point being the live value. The as\-of rule is load\-bearing: the headline formula says "*today* minus the oldest lot's date," which has no meaning at a historical point, and anchoring January to its 1st, 15th or 31st gives 0, 12 or 28 days from identical data. Calendar\-month boundaries are what §3.9 already fixes as the app's single period definition. **How the line is drawn (Decided, 2026\-09\-15):** in `acc`, with a dot on the last point (today), the six month initials under the points in `txt2`, and a `bordStrong` hairline at zero only where the values cross it — the device critique found the line drawn in `txt` with nothing to read it against, a decoration rather than a chart. `chartGeometry()` places the points and is tested. The same drawing serves §5.3. Points earlier than the opening lot are omitted rather than drawn as zero, so a two\-month\-old install shows two points, not six. Everything is replayed from the append\-only log, never from stored snapshots.

**The prospective toggle — a genuinely different question (Decided; window specified 2026\-09\-02).** A separate, optional "how long would this cover you" reading answers today's Savings balance ÷ a typical recent daily spend, and is kept explicitly distinct from an emergency\-fund Goal, which is a planning decision rather than something derived from past spending. The two numbers will not usually match, and that's intentional: one describes a pattern, the other a snapshot.

- **Daily spend \= the total of all Savings\-decreasing Transactions over the trailing three complete calendar months ÷ the number of days in that span.** Three complete months rather than a trailing 30 days specifically because §3.11 front\-loads every recurring sweep onto the 1st: a 30\-day window straddling a sweep halves the answer overnight, showing 300 days on the 31st and 75 days on the 1st for a person whose habits didn't change. A whole\-month window always contains whole sweep cycles.
- **Goal contributions count** toward that outflow, consistent with §3.2's treatment of them as money that has left the available pool and with §3.6's "one mechanism, not a different rule per item type." The conservative reading is right here for the same reason it is in §3.2 — this figure answers "how long could I last," and assuming the person stops funding their goals would flatter the answer.
- **The toggle is hidden until three complete calendar months of ledger exist, and hidden when the trailing outflow is zero (specified 2026\-09\-02).** A fresh install has no complete months to average, and a zero denominator has no answer — neither case has a meaningful number to floor or cap, so the toggle simply isn't offered rather than showing a placeholder.
- **Floored at 0 days when Savings ≤ 0**, and hidden while `Savings.amount` is `null`. A negative balance would otherwise divide out to a negative day count, which isn't a meaningful answer to "how long would this cover you." Whether Savings is −€5 or −€5,000, the honest answer is the same.

This is the Reports **default sub\-view** (changed from Spending) specifically because Savings/Age\-of\-Money is the figure §3.7's whole naming decision is oriented around, and because it's the simplest possible first\-open experience — one headline number, nothing to parse.

### 5\.2 Spending (Decided; composition specified 2026\-09\-02)

A share breakdown of where the current calendar month's money went, each slice drawn with the same neutral bar treatment as everywhere else. **The denominator is the month's net outflow from Savings (Decided, 2026\-09\-02)** — previously the section named two contributors and left the total undefined, so one build's €1,220 denominator and another's €1,800 produced shares differing by half.

Slices: **each Recurring Expense** (at its apportioned share for bimonthly/yearly, not its face value — the sweep is what actually left Savings), **each Category**, **Goals** as one combined slice, and **Uncategorised** for every `key: savings` outflow that belongs to no label — general "\+" Money\-out entries and negative Savings full\-editor deltas alike. **Since 2026\-10\-09 no entry writes a new Uncategorised row** — Savings has no Money out (§3.6) and an imported outflow needs a target (§4.3) — so the slice holds only earlier rows and Savings\-editor corrections downward. **Spending from a Goal (`spend:<goalId>`) is no slice of its own:** it moves no Savings, and the money was counted, as the Goals slice, in the month it went into the Goal; counting it again when spent would make the breakdown stop reconciling.

**Savings\-*increasing* rows on a labeled key are netted against that key's own slice, not ignored and not counted as spending.** A −€25 Category relabel (§3.6), a −€50 Goal correction, a −€300 Recurring Expense deletion reversal (§3.11) and a deleted Goal's returned €800 all reduce their slice and the denominator together. Taken gross instead, a person who moved €50 into a goal once and corrected it would see €100 of Goals "spending," and a deleted goal's refund would be invisible — in the one report whose stated justification is that it reconciles to what actually left Savings.

Including Goals and Uncategorised at all is deliberate, for the same reason: a breakdown that reconciles is checkable, and one that quietly omits a €500 goal contribution and an €80 cash withdrawal invites the person to wonder where the difference went. It also follows §3.2's treatment of goal money as already spoken for. Goals stay a single slice rather than one per goal so a person saving toward several things doesn't see their spending report dominated by their saving.

**When any slice nets negative, the chart is suppressed and the month is shown as a plain list of each slice's net figure instead (Decided, 2026\-09\-02).** Deleting a Goal that accumulated €800 over earlier months, in a month with €1,000 of Category spending, gives a €200 denominator against a −€800 slice — so Categories would read 500% and the shares would describe nothing. A share chart is only meaningful when every part is a part of the whole; where it isn't, plain figures are the honest fallback, and they carry no verdict either (§2.2). The same treatment covers **a month whose net outflow is zero or negative overall**, which shows the plain list plus a line noting that nothing net left Savings.

The month is the calendar month per §3.9, and rows are attributed by each Transaction's `date` (§3.6), so a backdated entry lands in the month it really happened.

### 5\.3 Savings vs. Owed (Decided, 2026\-07\-23; renamed from "Net worth" 2026\-07\-28; derivability closed 2026\-09\-02)

**Renamed deliberately, not just relabeled (Decided, 2026\-07\-28).** The underlying math is unchanged — what changed is the name it's presented under. "Net worth" carries a cultural weight this app's whole register (§2.1, §2.2) otherwise works against: it invites reading a number substantially shaped by circumstance as a verdict on a person. Rather than cut the feature — its actual utility (seeing Savings and debt move together) is real, particularly for anyone actively paying down a Loan while building Savings — the fix follows the same pattern already used for Buffer: keep the number, remove the verdict.

**Off by default, toggle in Settings (§4.7).** ~~Six months~~ **`trendMonths` months (§4.6, default 6; corrected 2026\-09\-25)** of history plus the live total, computed as **Savings minus the sum of all Account balances** — Accounts are Credit and Loan only, both liabilities, so they subtract rather than add. A dip is drawn no differently than a rise — deliberately tested against a dataset that actually dips, not just one that trends upward. Defaulting off reflects that a person with no Credit or Loan accounts would just see Savings again under a different name.

**The Savings side is unallocated `Savings.amount`, so money held in Goals is not counted (§3.2 — confirmed on review, 2026\-09\-02).** Someone with €5,000 fully allocated to Goals and a €3,000 loan reads −€3,000 here. That reading is intended and it is the accurate one, for reasons specific to this report rather than inherited wholesale from §3.2:

- **A loan and an emergency fund are not the same kind of obligation and don't net against each other in real life.** A loan is a planned, long\-term commitment on a set and regular cadence — a Recurring Expense the app sweeps on schedule (§3.11, §9.1). An emergency fund exists for the unplanned and irregular: a sudden medical expense, not a scheduled payment. The emergency fund is not available to pay the loan down, and showing a single netted figure that implies it is would misdescribe both.
- **Netting them would also double\-count the same euro** — once in Savings and once in the Goal — leaving the person with the impression they hold more than they do, at exactly the moment they're looking at a report about what they owe.
- §3.2's second argument applies here too: an emergency fund only stays available if it is never presented as spendable room. Reading as already spent is the mechanism, not a side effect.
