Requesting on arrival — the 2026\-09\-14 wording — meant the OS dialog opened over the page the instant it appeared, so the one sentence that explains the request was only ever seen dimmed behind the dialog (2026\-09\-15 device critique); `PermissionStepTest` holds the page still until the tap.
3. **Tutorial** — a live, in\-context overlay tour of the real app (§11.6), with a single persistent Skip button. Skipping at any point and completing every stop both lead to the same next step — there's no shortened or degraded path to design around.
4. **Settings, landing on Currency** — expanded/visible rather than sitting collapsed in a list (§4.7), defaulting to **€**. **Since §4.7 became a hub, this is a deep link into the Appearance & accessibility screen rather than a landing on the hub itself (2026\-09\-04)** — the requirement was always that Currency be *visible on arrival*, and a hub row reading "Appearance & accessibility →" that has to be tapped is precisely the collapsed\-in\-a\-list case this step rules out. **How the person leaves it (Decided, 2026\-09\-14 — the deep link had no way onward, and the prototype's separate one\-field page contradicted it):** it is the real Appearance & accessibility screen, with the prototype's sentence above it — *"What currency should amounts show in? You can change it anytime in Settings."* — and a **"Continue to Sci\-Fin"** button at the bottom in place of the back chevron. A second Profile (§8.3) lands on the same screen the same way. **Currency leads here, and only here (Decided, 2026\-09\-15):** the sentence asks about currency, so the Currency row is the first thing under it; the LOOK grid and the other rows follow in §4.7's order, which the settings hub keeps unchanged. Before this the Currency row sat third, under LOOK and Appearance — *visible on arrival* in letter, buried in fact (2026\-09\-15 device critique). The order is data, `appearanceSections()`, and tested.
5. **Homepage** (Budget → Envelopes) — Savings is not asked for anywhere in this flow (§11.7); the person discovers it themselves.

This replaces the original implementation (Welcome → Appearance → mandatory Savings entry → Finish) entirely — Savings entry is no longer a gated onboarding step at all.

**Onboarding belongs to the Profile, and screen 1 is where a Profile is born (Decided, 2026\-09\-14).** On the very first open there is no Profile yet: the app opens on screen 1 with nothing behind the sheet but Budget home's empty ground, and Continue creates the first Profile with the name typed and the theme chosen. Nothing is auto\-created and nothing is called "Mine" — the Stage 1 build's silent `AppSession.open` behaviour (create a Profile named "Mine", then mark onboarding incomplete) is superseded and is corrected in Stage 2 (§14). **Creating a second Profile (§8.3) runs the same screen 1 for the same purpose — its name and its theme — then step 4, Settings landing on Currency, then that Profile's Budget home.** Steps 2 and 3 do not repeat: the notification permission is the device's, already answered, and the tutorial is replayable from Settings (§11.6) for anyone who wants it on a new pool. So `onboardingComplete` (§12) is a per\-Profile flag inside §8.4's wrap, set by screen 1's Continue for a second Profile and by step 5's arrival for the first; a Profile restored from a backup (§4.7) carries its own value and is never re\-onboarded.

**Screen 1 offers a restore (Decided, 2026\-09\-23).** Under Continue sits a quiet second line — *“Restore a backup”* — opening §4.7's picker and its review card directly; on Confirm the restored Profile's Budget home replaces the flow entirely. Without it the most common moment anyone needs a restore — a reinstall, a new phone — costs a Profile invented only to be overwritten a minute later, which is precisely what the device walk of 2026\-09\-23 had to do to reach the restore at all. A line and not a second button: screen 1's one job is still the Profile being born, and this is the exception that proves it.

**An interrupted flow resumes where it stopped (Decided, 2026\-09\-23).** Screen 1's Continue creates the Profile, so a person whose app is killed between screen 1 and step 5 holds a Profile with `onboardingComplete` still false — and, before this, no route back: the next open landed on Budget home, steps 2 to 5 never ran, and because the flag is one\-way and only step 5 sets it, §11.3's prompt could never become eligible for that Profile. Nothing crashed and nothing was lost; a step of the flow and a later prompt simply never happened, silently, which is the shape of defect §2.3 least tolerates. The step reached is therefore stored beside the flag (§12) and the next open resumes at it. Found on the device on 2026\-09\-23, by a reinstall that killed the process mid\-flow.

### 11\.2 Why the goal\-name step was removed (Decided)

Asking for a goal before the app knows what someone has saved means guessing at a plan with nothing real to calculate against — the app had no basis for a realistic timeline or contribution suggestion at that point in the flow. A goal is now only ever proposed *after* Savings is known.

### 11\.3 Post\-onboarding goal prompt (Decided, implemented; "not on Skip" caveat retired 2026\-07\-24; gating added 2026\-09\-02)

A gentle, dismissible prompt appears **once**\: *"Is there something in particular you'd like to save up for?"* — accepting opens the full Goal calculator; declining ("Not right now") simply dismisses it. The original "not on Skip" caveat no longer applies now that skipping and completing the tutorial converge on the same next step (§11.1).

**It waits until Savings has a real logged value (Decided, 2026\-09\-02 — resolves a collision the 2026\-07\-24 redesign created and never re\-reasoned).** §11.2 states the governing rule — "a goal is now only ever proposed *after* Savings is known" — and gives the reason: with nothing real to calculate against, the calculator has no basis for a realistic timeline or contribution. But §11.7 then removed the Savings step entirely, so at §11.1's step 5 Savings is by construction `null`. Firing the prompt there would take a brand\-new person straight into §6.4's calculator with no Savings figure, where the sustainability view has nothing to compute from and the app's first substantive interaction is a line about the cushion they don't have — the precise register failure §2.4 exists to prevent. So the prompt is deferred to the first arrival at Budget home *after* `Savings.amount` becomes non\-null, in the person's own time, consistent with §11.7's whole stance.

**Shown at most once, tracked by `goalPromptDismissed` (§12, added 2026\-09\-02).** "Appears once" needs a stored flag or it isn't once — without one the prompt returns on every arrival at Budget home, turning a one\-time invitation into a recurring nudge, which §2.2/§2.3 rule out. Set when the prompt is accepted or declined. "Replay onboarding" (§4.7) does not clear it, consistent with that feature's "resetting no data either way."

### 11\.4 Check\-in day / reminders (Decided, 2026\-07\-19 — resolved via §3.9)

The original check\-in\-day picker is dropped from onboarding entirely, and stays dropped — no day\-of\-week concept returns anywhere. In its place, the **New Month notification** (§3.9) fires unconditionally on the 1st of each month, riding the calendar\-month boundary Categories already need rather than requiring its own onboarding step or day\-picker. This is a real notification\-system commitment (permission handling, boot recovery), ~~not yet built beyond the reviewer\-only demo button (§4.7)~~ compiled and wired 2026\-09\-14 (§13.3), but the design question itself is settled. **Due\-date reminders for individual Recurring Expenses are resolved separately** (§3.11) — a per\-item due date and preset lead time, distinct from this monthly\-boundary notification, not something this section needs to cover.

### 11\.5 Notification permission timing (Decided, 2026\-07\-24; platform scope added 2026\-09\-02)

Requested right after Theme selection, before the Tutorial begins — a genuine OS\-level system dialog, not an in\-app toggle, so there's no in\-app skip to design: it resolves to Allow or Deny on its own terms. This is a deliberate early placement rather than the "ask in context" pattern the app otherwise favors (§3.3's anchor\-date design, §11.2's reasoning) — the trade\-off was made consciously, keeping the flow a single fixed sequence rather than deferring the prompt to some later, harder\-to\-predict moment.

**Step 2 fires only where the platform has a runtime permission prompt (Decided, 2026\-09\-02).** Android does; **Windows 10/11 does not** — notifications are enabled per\-app by default there and disabled from system Settings, with no runtime dialog to request. On Windows the step is skipped entirely rather than rendered as an in\-app stand\-in, and `notificationPermissionGranted` (§12) is read from the system notification setting for the app instead of from a dialog answer. §4.7's plain line beneath the Notifications toggle then behaves identically on both platforms: it appears whenever the OS\-level answer is negative, however that answer was arrived at. This was the one place §1's two\-platform scope left an onboarding step undefined on one of its targets.

**When a notification does not fire (Decided, 2026\-09\-14).** The scheduling subsystem is inexact by construction — WorkManager on Android delivers inside a window around §12's 09:00 target, not at it, and the app deliberately asks for no exact\-alarm permission (§14) — and an OEM's battery manager can drop the work entirely. What each miss costs is therefore stated, so that none of it is discovered as a surprise:

- **A missed New Month notification loses only the notification.** §3.9's catch\-up runs on the next open and does the sweep with the right dates; the figures are unaffected. The notification was always a courtesy, never the mechanism.
- **A missed due\-date reminder (§3.11) is not re\-raised later.** A reminder that arrives after the date it was warning about is a reproach, not a reminder (§2.3); the sweep has already handled the money either way.
- **A missed import reminder (§4.3) likewise.** The next month's fires as scheduled.
- **Permission revoked or switched off at the OS level** is shown plainly under the Notifications toggle (§4.7) and nothing else changes — the in\-app preferences keep their values so that re\-granting restores exactly what was set.
- **After a reboot**, the whole schedule is re\-armed from the declarative reminder list (`syncReminders`, §14) rather than from anything remembered about individual alarms; there is nothing to reconcile because nothing was stored per\-alarm.
- **The app does not ask for the battery\-optimisation exemption.** A sibling project that runs alarm\-clock\-grade reminders does; Sci\-Fin's reminders are none of them time\-critical, the dialog is one more interruption at first run (§11.1), and the prompt is discouraged for Play distribution.

**The Windows seam, walked on the machine (2026\-09\-15).** Every piece was exercised against the real OS, and four of them were defective in ways no test had reached — each fixed and re\-verified the same morning, the tests that drive the real OS kept behind `SCIFIN_WALK=1` so the acceptance build never touches it:

- **Reminder bodies with a currency symbol never registered.** The registration script is handed to Windows PowerShell as a file; without a byte\-order mark it is read as ANSI, "€" arrives as `â‚¬`, and `‚` is a quote to the parser — the script died silently. The New Month copy carries no currency, which is why it alone worked. The file now carries a BOM; a "€900" reminder registered through the seam fired at its minute with no app process alive.
- **The permission read always said denied.** The WinRT notifier's `Setting` property is empty through PowerShell's projection for every app, registered or not. The read now comes from the registry where the Settings toggles live (the global `NOC_GLOBAL_SETTING_TOASTS_ENABLED` and the per\-app `Enabled`; absent means on).
- **Neither Task Scheduler entry was ever created** — once by omission (nothing called the installer), then by quoting: `schtasks /TR` wants the quoted executable and its argument in one value, and the JVM's Windows argument quoting wraps a value that begins with a quote in a second pair. The entries now go through `Register-ScheduledTask`, executable and argument as separate values, and the first normal launch of the packaged executable installs them when the daily one is absent; a development launcher (`java.exe`) never does.
- **Toasts from the app's AppUserModelID were accepted and never shown** — no error, no history entry. A per\-user `HKCU\\Software\\Classes\\AppUserModelId` entry and a process\-level AUMID were each tried and neither sufficed; a Start Menu shortcut carrying `System.AppUserModel.ID` did, on its own. jpackage's MSI writes a shortcut without the property, so the app writes its own on first launch through the shell property store. This is the same rule Electron documents for its apps: a Start Menu shortcut with the AUMID, which its installers set and a development build must set by hand.
- **`--sync-notifications` was accepted by nothing:** `main` took no arguments, so the headless top\-up the two entries invoke would have opened the window. It now re\-arms from stored state and exits in about a second, writing what it did to `launch.log` in the data folder since the packaged launcher has no console. Verified both by hand and with Task Scheduler itself starting the entry: the queue read back identically (the twelve New Month toasts, plus a reminder where an expense has one).

One observation stays open rather than resolved: a database seeded from a test process that exited without closing it — its rows only in the write\-ahead log — was readable by hand\-started runs and empty to Scheduler\-started ones, and the rows were later lost when the launcher's SQLite checkpointed nothing. It did not reproduce with a database created and closed by the app itself, and the unsafe act was the seeding; it is recorded here so that the next person to see it knows it has been seen.

What the walk did not do: wait for a New Month toast to fire on the 1st (the reminder that fired used the identical registration path), and reboot the machine to see the logon entry fire on its own trigger (it was started by the Scheduler on demand instead).

**The Android seam, walked on the device (2026\-09\-23).** The OnePlus 9 Pro on Android 14, with the acceptance build installed: §4.7's export and restore and §4.3's statement import driven through the real Storage Access Framework, and the phone actually rebooted. What it settled:

- **The backup round trip is exact.** The exported file's counts matched the database row for row (2 Profiles, 5 Categories, 3 Goals, 6 Recurring Expenses, 43 Transactions, 2 Accounts); `pm clear` was then run, the app came up on a cold first run with an empty register, and the review card named the file's own figures — *2 profiles · 43 transactions · Alex · Trust* — before anything changed. After Confirm every count was back, `onboardingComplete` with it, and Budget home matched the capture taken before the wipe.
- **A non\-ASCII payee survives every hop.** A statement row reading *Caffè Verdi* was read through the SAF stream, written to Room and exported again as `c3 a8` unchanged, with no byte\-order mark on the JSON. Worth stating because the Windows walk's worst defect was exactly this class in the other direction, and because a backup of ASCII\-only data proves nothing about it — the first export here was pure ASCII and its clean bill of health meant nothing until a payee with an accent was pushed through.
- **The import review does what §4.3 says.** Of six rows: one already\-imported row came back DUPLICATE on its synthetic key *after a restore*, which is the evidence that `importState` round\-trips through the backup file; one matched a Recurring Expense by name and came back RECURRING; `1.234,56` parsed as 1234.56 and `03/04/2026` as 3 April, both shown on the card before any write. A vetoed row wrote nothing at all, a row reassigned away from its default landed in the Category chosen and taught the payee rule, and Savings moved by exactly the arithmetic of the three confirmed rows and no more.
- **The reboot re\-arms nothing, because nothing needed re\-arming.** Read out of WorkManager's own queue (`no_backup/androidx.work.workdb`) rather than out of `dumpsys`, which names no work, both entries survived the reboot with their row ids, enqueue times and fire times identical, with the app's UI never opened. Opening the app then replaced both with new rows carrying the same fire times — `restoreAfterBoot` converging on the same answer, which is what §14 claims for it and what had never been measured.

**And one defect that had nothing to do with the seam.** The tour's spotlight was drawn one status bar below the element it described, because `tutAnchor` records the anchor in the window's root coordinates while the overlay draws inside §4.8's frame, which is padded clear of the system bars. The same offset put stop 2's Skip and Next inside the navigation bar, where a tap goes to the system: on first run the tour could be neither advanced nor skipped, and reopening the app returned to the same stop. It is a regression from the edge\-to\-edge frame added on 2026\-09\-15, after Stage 2's tutorial walk — fixed the same day, with §11.6 recording the rule it broke.

What this walk did not do: wait for either notification to fire on its own date, and exercise OFX — the CSV path shares `annotate` and `confirm` with it, but not the parser.

`newMonthNotificationEnabled` (§12) defaults **true** regardless of what's answered here — if the OS permission is denied or switched off, the in\-app preference stays on but nothing displays, which §4.7 surfaces plainly rather than leaving the toggle silently inert. The same permission covers §3.11's per\-expense due\-date reminders, which ride the same scheduling subsystem (§3.9).

### 11\.6 Tutorial: live overlay, not a scripted sequence (Decided, 2026\-07\-24; copy corrected 2026\-07\-29; stop count, navigation and replay specified 2026\-09\-02)

**Onboarding's tour is a slideshow; the live overlay moves to Settings as an index (Decided and built 2026\-10\-27, owner).** A stop's window had to fit beside the section it pointed at, so a long line was cut to fit the space left over (the Recurring Expenses stop, 330 characters in Italian). Step 3 of §11.1 now shows the ten stops as **six slides**: Savings + Goals · Income · Recurring Expenses · Categories + Accounts · Learn + Reports · Appearance + Profiles — short stops in pairs, the longest alone. Each stop on a slide is a **live miniature** of its section: the real screen, drawn in the Profile's own theme, language and data and cut to the section its `tutAnchor` marks, with the stop's place (*Budget › Goals*) above it and its copy under it — no image files, so nothing goes stale and nothing needs a copy per language or theme. A miniature takes no taps and a screen reader hears the slide's copy, not the screen inside it. **Skip**, progress dots and **Next** / **Done** sit in a fixed foot, at the same height on every slide (owner, 2026\-10\-27), while the slide above it scrolls; Back steps to the previous slide, and before the first it ends the tour, as Skip does. Each stop's mockup substitution (below) still applies, drawn as an illustrative line under the miniature. **Settings' Tour row** opens an index under it, one link per stop; a link goes to that stop's own screen with its window over the section — the live overlay below, one stop at a time, with **Done** in place of Next and Skip — and **Watch the slideshow** replays the slides. The condensed static summary and *"Replay the full tour"* are gone: the index is both. The window rules below (placement, the frame, the mockup substitution) now govern that one\-stop window.

**Mechanism:** a real\-time overlay on the actual, live app — not a separate sequence of mocked screens. Each stop is a single small message window anchored to real UI, and the person is free to interact with the genuine app underneath before moving to the next stop. Chosen deliberately over a scripted screen sequence: walking through the same number of concepts as a series of full mock screens would read as an unbroken wall of information, where the same content as small, in\-context windows over the real interface doesn't. Where the real screen is genuinely empty, a single small mockup item is shown *within the overlay window itself* — illustrative only, not written to real state — rather than either skipping the concept or scripting a whole fake screen around it.

**Navigation and state (specified 2026\-09\-02 — the tour crosses five tabs and one Settings section, and who drives was never stated).** Advancing with **Next** navigates the app to the next stop's screen. The person's own taps are permitted and simply change the current screen; the overlay window follows rather than fighting them, and Next resumes the sequence by navigating to where the next stop lives. **Anything the person actually enters during the tour is real, persisted state** — the "not written to real state" carve\-out applies only to the mockup items drawn inside an overlay window, never to the live app underneath. So a person who logs a genuine Savings figure at stop 1 has logged it: §11.7's message clears, and §11.3's prompt becomes eligible at step 5.

**One persistent Skip button**, pinned at the bottom of every window rather than a separate control on each. Skipping at any point, or finishing every stop, both land on the same next step (§11.1's step 4).

**The window is placed in the overlay's own space, and never leaves the frame (Decided by defect, 2026\-09\-23).** Anchors are recorded in the window's root coordinates, which begin behind the system bars; the overlay draws inside §4.8's frame, which is padded clear of them. The two have to be reconciled before anything is drawn — the overlay subtracts its own origin — or every ring sits one status bar below what it describes. And the window's *measured* height decides which side of the ring it takes: a fixed guess at that height put a five\-line stop below a ring with no room beneath it, which on a phone with a three\-button navigation bar drew Skip and Next where a tap reaches the system instead of the app. Both were true on the device on 2026\-09\-23; `TutorialWindowTest` now holds the placement inside the frame at every anchor position, which is the half of it a desktop test can see.

**Eight stops, live\-overlay (reduced from nine, 2026\-09\-02):** **Ten since 2026\-10\-09** — Profiles returned when §8 shipped (below), and stop 3 was split (owner: *"Split it, same words"*) into a **Goals** stop on the Goals section, now second, and a **Recurring Expenses** stop on its own section, after Income, each keeping its line word for word. The order is Savings → Goals → Income → Recurring Expenses → Categories → Activity → Learn → Reports → Appearance → Profiles.

1. **Envelopes → Savings row** (illustrative mockup figure substituted in, since real Savings is unset): *"Savings is what you have available. It will never display an inflated number."*
2. **Envelopes → Income section** — shown temporarily via the same mockup\-item mechanism as Savings, even though `incomeEnabled` (§12) stays false throughout; the section reverts to its normal conditional rendering the moment the tour exits. ~~*"Off by default, you can turn it on in Settings and choose between manual and automated recurring deposits."*~~ *"Off by default, you can turn it on in Settings: a set amount, deposited for you or logged by hand, or pay by the hour, logged as you work it."* (owner, 2026\-10\-26, for §3.10)
3. **Envelopes → Recurring Expenses / Goals section**, one window covering both, kept as two separate lines within it since they work differently — Recurring Expenses is automatic, Goals is not: *"Recurring Expenses: your flat\-fee bills. What's due this month leaves Savings on the 1st, up front; anything billed less often is set aside a little each month — no surprise charges."* / *"Goals: near or far. Add to one whenever you like, like a piggy bank."* **Tightened (Decided, 2026\-09\-15)** from a wording twice this length that took ten lines on a 360dp phone — twice any other stop (device critique); both facts of the sweep survive, and `TutorialStopThreeTest` caps the stop's length.
   - **Corrected 2026\-09\-02.** The previous line — *"Detracts its full amount from Savings every 1st of the month"* — stated the mechanic wrongly in both directions: a biweekly expense takes two or three times its amount in a given month (§3.11's deliberate real count), and a yearly one takes a twelfth, not its full amount. Teaching the wrong model of the app's single most surprising mechanic, in the one place designed to prevent surprise, is worse than saying less; the replacement covers both branches without naming a cadence.
4. **Categories tab:** *"What you set aside and what you spent. Categories frame where all purchases fit, month after month. There are no one\-off entries — they're pieces of the puzzle."* The person has to find or create a durable category before logging a purchase — a deliberate structural choice (categories persist indefinitely, only `spent` resets monthly, §3.9), not an enforcement rule about what's "allowed."
5. **Activity tab:** *"A view of credit cards and loans, to keep the payments in check."*
6. **Learn tab:** *"The science behind the app and its features. If something needs explaining, you can read about it here."*
7. **Reports tab:** *"Here you find visual and written summaries of how money moves over time."*
8. **Settings → Appearance & accessibility:** ~~*"Accessibility can be personalized by text size, density, reduced motion, and low\-distraction mode."*~~ *"Accessibility can be personalized by density, reduced motion, and low\-distraction mode — text follows your phone's own size."* (Decided, 2026\-09\-25, with the owner, with the setting's removal) **Next navigates into that sub\-screen, not to the Settings hub (2026\-09\-04)** — §4.7 made Appearance a spoke, and this stop teaches the controls themselves rather than the row that leads to them, so anchoring to the row would leave the tour pointing at a label instead of at the real thing, which is the one premise §11.6 does not bend.

**The Profiles stop is dropped until §8 is built (Decided, 2026\-09\-02).** A ninth stop taught Profiles by anchoring to "Settings → Profiles" — a surface that exists in no other section, pointing at a control §8.3 describes as "currently inert." A live\-overlay tour whose premise is anchoring to the real app cannot teach a control that does nothing when tapped. §4.5 sets the precedent for deferred features: they are listed as planned, not taught. The stop returns, with its existing copy (*"For money that has to stay separate — someone else's, something held for someone else, or your own but bound by different rules, like an inheritance"*), when §8 ships.

**Replay, from Settings (§4.7):** ~~a condensed, static summary — not a live re\-run — plus a button to replay the full live\-overlay tour~~ **the Tour index and the slideshow (2026\-10\-27, above)**, resetting no data either way. **On replay, each stop's mockup substitution is conditional on the underlying value still being unset (specified 2026\-09\-02)** — every stop above was written for a first\-run, empty app, and a person replaying usually has real data. Stop 1 shows their real Savings row where `Savings.amount` is non\-null, and stop 2 shows their real Income section where `incomeEnabled` is true, falling back to the mockup item only where the value genuinely is still unset. This is §11.6's own rule — "where the real screen is genuinely empty" — applied as a runtime condition rather than a first\-run assumption; without it, a replay pastes fictional figures over a person's real ones, which is exactly the seeded\-demo\-data failure §14 exists to prevent. **The stop\-2 revert triggers on tour exit** (skip or final stop), not on "onboarding ends" — `onboardingComplete` is a one\-way flag (§12) and never flips back, so on a replay that trigger would never fire and the Income section would stay stuck in its mockup rendering.

### 11\.7 Savings entry is discovered, not collected (Decided, 2026\-07\-24; condition specified 2026\-09\-02)

No step anywhere in onboarding asks for a Savings figure, and none enforces one. Once the person reaches Budget home (§11.1's step 5), Savings behaves exactly as it does on any other visit — the quick\-add buttons are sitting right there, and the figure stays unset until the person adds it themselves, in their own time.

**While `Savings.amount` is `null` (§3.1), a small, non\-blocking message appears under the Savings row** noting that the app's core functions depend on that figure being real — informational only, never a gate, consistent with §2.3's non\-blocking stance and the same instinct that removed the mandatory goal\-name step (§11.2). **The condition is `null`, not zero (specified 2026\-09\-02).** Testing "has a real logged value" as a non\-zero amount would leave the message permanently on screen for someone whose savings genuinely are €0 — telling a person in real distress that their honest answer doesn't count, which is precisely the failure §1 and §2.4 are built against. A deliberate zero is a real answer and clears the message like any other value.

Prior context for what Savings actually means (not a bank\-account balance, never inflated) has already been given by the tutorial (§11.6, stop 1) by the time the person reaches this point, so the field itself doesn't need to carry that explanation alone.

## 12\. Data Model Sketch

**Status: Decided as to shape, completeness and — since 2026\-09\-14 — storage form.** The engineering questions this paragraph used to leave to §13.3 are settled by the Stage 1 native build (§14): **money is a `Long` in minor units** everywhere in the engine, with only the display layer formatting (the prototype's floating point and `toFixed(2)` are gone; §6.1's and §3.11's round\-ups are integer `ceilDiv`); **entity ids are UUID strings**, unique across Profiles by construction (§8.4); **`date` fields are calendar days stored as ISO text and `loggedAt` is an instant stored as epoch milliseconds** (the *Types of time* rule below, made concrete); the store is **Room, one file, every table keyed by `profileId`** rather than a database per Profile, so one schema version and one backup cover every pool; and the engine works on an in\-memory `ProfileData` — one Profile's whole world — loaded and saved whole, which is §8.4's swap\-in/swap\-out as one object and what lets every rule below be tested without a database. Originally: *Decided as to shape and completeness, not as to storage form (2026\-09\-02).* Every field below is required by a Decided section, and every piece of state those sections' mechanics need is present — audited field\-by\-field in both directions this pass, three times. What remains open is engineering\-level: types, indexing, migration, and how this maps onto a real store; that is logged in §13.3 rather than left implicit, since the previous "not a ratified design" status was vague enough for §13 to overlook it.

**Types of time (Decided, 2026\-09\-14).** Two kinds of time appear in this model and they are not interchangeable. **`date` fields** (`Transaction.date`, `dueDate`, `targetDate`, `incomePayDate`, `lastImportDate`, every "last … month" marker) are **calendar days**: timezone\-naive, written `YYYY\-MM\-DD`, the device\-local day at the moment the event happened or was entered. **`loggedAt`** is an **instant**: a real UTC timestamp. The consequences are the point of stating it: §7.3's 48\-hour pair window and its "immediately following in `loggedAt` order" rule are instant arithmetic and unaffected by where the device is; §3.9's month boundary and §3.11's sweep fire at **device\-local midnight of the 1st**, so a person who travels does not get a New Month early or late relative to their own day; and changing time zone never re\-files history, because a calendar day carries no zone to re\-interpret. The prototype's `today()` is the device\-local day. §4.3's import keeps the statement's own day as given, since a bank statement is already a calendar\-day document.

**Storage version (Decided, 2026\-09\-14).** The persisted root carries **`schemaVersion`**, an integer starting at **1**, and a backup file (§4.7) carries the same number. A reader that meets a file whose version is **higher than its own refuses it whole** rather than half\-reading what it recognises — the failure a version field exists to prevent is not a crash, it is a silent partial load that looks like a successful one. A file with no version predates the field and is read as 1. ~~**Migration policy is phased:** before the first release anyone uses daily, a schema change may be destructive, because nothing real is stored that the person cannot re\-enter and hand\-written migrations against a shape still moving are waste; from that release on, every bump ships a migration, and the backup file is the recovery path when one fails. The switch\-over point is the release, not a date.~~

**The switch\-over happened on 2026\-09\-24, before any release, because the condition it was written against expired.** The phased policy above was conditional, not chronological — destructive changes were admissible *because nothing real is stored that the person cannot re\-enter*. That stopped being true at the 2026\-09\-23 device walks (§11.5), which were run against the owner's own profile on their own phone, and the Windows build carries the same profile: on 2026\-09\-24 the desktop database held 350KB of data. A policy tied to a condition switches when the condition changes, and the release was only ever a proxy for it. **From here every `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump ships a migration.**

**What that means concretely (Decided, 2026\-09\-24).**

1. **Every shipped schema has a path.** `Migration(1, 2)` and `Migration(2, 3)` are written and wired through `addMigrations`; 1→2 adds `settings.onboardingStep` and 2→3 adds `settings.trendMonths`, both single additive columns (and 3→4, 2026\-09\-25, adds §3.9's stored card: three more) — which is the shape every bump ~~so far has~~ **before 2026\-09\-25** had; **3→4 also drops the recurring expenses' last\-fired field and 4→5 drops the text scale, both by rebuilding the table, since API 30's SQLite has no `DROP COLUMN`** (added later the same day), and the reason the omission cost nothing until now. `shared/schemas/` already holds the exported schema JSON for each version, so a migration is checked against the real shape rather than a remembered one.
2. **A test fails when a bump has no path.** The suite reads `shared/schemas/` and refuses to go green if `SCHEMA_VERSION` has risen without a corresponding migration. The failure mode this prevents — a forgotten migration reaching a device — is one no amount of care reliably prevents on its own.
3. **The database is snapshotted before it is opened at a new version.** When the stored version differs from `SCHEMA_VERSION`, the app writes a snapshot **before** Room touches the file. **The version is read through SQLite, not from the file header (corrected 2026\-09\-24, by the test).** The first design read `user_version` from **byte offset 60** of the file, on the reasoning that four bytes need no database connection and so cannot fail on a file Room refuses. The test showed why that is not enough: a `PRAGMA user_version = 2` can still be sitting in the write\-ahead log, leaving byte 60 reading **0**. The header is therefore **at or behind the truth, never ahead**. Behind is mostly harmless — it snapshots when it need not — but not in one direction: on a **downgrade**, where a newer build wrote the log and the header still reads the current version, the snapshot would be skipped and the destructive fallback would drop exactly what this exists to protect. So `user_version` is read through raw SQLite, which opens a schema Room would refuse, with the header kept only as a fallback and an unreadable result treated as *snapshot anyway*.
4. **`fallbackToDestructiveMigration` is kept, and is no longer silent loss.** With rule 1 there is no ordinary path to it and with rule 3 anything it drops still exists on disk. Removing it would turn an unforeseen gap into a crash before any window is created — `store.start()` runs before `application {}` on the desktop — which is a worse failure than opening and pointing at a snapshot.

5. **A store SQLite cannot read is moved aside before anything opens it, and the person is asked what next (Decided, 2026\-09\-25, with the owner — closes §13.1 items 7 and 8).** Measured from the driver sources that day: `AndroidSQLiteDriver` opens through Android's default error handler, which **deletes** a database that reports corruption — and rule 3's version read opened the file through it, so the snapshot meant to protect the file would have erased it. `BundledSQLiteDriver` deletes nothing, but it throws, before any window exists. So each platform first runs `PRAGMA quick_check` through an open that cannot delete; a file that fails it, or cannot be opened at all, is renamed with its `-wal` and `-shm` into `snapshots/` as `scifin-damaged-<time>-<device>.db`, and a marker beside the store names it. The app then opens empty and, ahead of §11.1's screen 1, shows one sheet with no dismiss: *"Sci\-Fin can't read its data"*, *"The file has been kept as a copy."*, and two buttons — **Restore a backup** (§4.7's pick and review) and **Start fresh** (screen 1). Either answer clears the marker; the copy stays. **Not covered, and stated so it is not assumed:** corruption that first surfaces while the app is running still meets Android's default handler inside Room's own connection — the driver takes no handler, and the open\-helper route would contend with Room for `user_version`.

**The snapshot is one file, and that is the whole design (Decided, 2026\-09\-24).** It is written with SQLite's `VACUUM INTO`, not copied: the live store is **`scifin.db` plus `-wal` and `-shm`**, and on 2026\-09\-24 the main file held 4KB against a 350KB write\-ahead log — so a copy of `scifin.db` alone would capture a header and no data, and a copy of all three is three files that must stay mutually consistent. `VACUUM INTO` folds the log in and emits **one complete, consistent database**, through raw SQLite, so it works on a schema Room refuses to validate — which is exactly the pre\-migration case. Snapshots are **immutable and device\-qualified** — `scifin-v<N>-<host>.db`, in a **`snapshots/` subfolder** beside the store. **The name carries no timestamp, deliberately (2026\-09\-24):** one snapshot per version per device, written once and never rewritten. That bounds them, which a timestamp would not — a stale header read would write a fresh snapshot on every launch, forever — and it means a known\-good snapshot is never replaced by a later, possibly worse one. Those properties are what make them safe next to file sync: one file rather than three, written once and never modified, and named so two machines can never collide. **They are excluded from sync by default** and the app's data folder is not a sync share; the one\-line exclusion is `/snapshots` in a Syncthing `.stignore`. A snapshot is a local undo for a bad upgrade — the artefact meant to *travel* between devices is §4.7's JSON backup, which is a single text file, version\-tolerant by `ignoreUnknownKeys` plus field defaults, and validated whole before it is applied. The live database is never synced, for the reason §4.7 already gives: *two Profiles with the same id from two devices have no correct merge*.

**Single\-source values (added 2026\-09\-14).** Every tunable number in this document, with the section that owns its reasoning and the name it carries in code. A value changes **here first**; the owning section may restate it for readability but this table is what the code is checked against.

| Value | Owner | In code |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Pair\-deletion window: **48 h**, measured on `loggedAt` | §7.3 | `PAIR_WINDOW` in `Ledger.kt` (the prototype's name was `PAIR_WINDOW_MS`; corrected 2026\-09\-25) |
| Notification delivery hour: **09:00** device\-local, a target inside WorkManager's window, not a promise | §11.5 | `NOTIFY_HOUR` (shared engine since 2026\-09\-14; both platform schedulers read it) |
| Due\-date reminder lead times: **1 \| 3 \| 7 \| 14** days | §3.11 | `reminderLeadDays` |
| Import reminder day: the **1st** of each month | §4.3 | `importReminderDay` |
| Recurring\-expense threshold: **1/12 of Savings** with Income off; **1/3 of `incomeReceivedThisMonth`** with Income on | §3.5 | — |
| Credit\-card minimum payment: **2 %** of balance, **floor 25** in the display currency | §9.2 | `MIN_PAYMENT_PCT`, `MIN_PAYMENT_FLOOR` |
| Buffer report's coverage toggle: hidden until **three complete calendar months** of ledger exist | §5.1 | — |
| ~~Text scale: **0\.875 / 1 / 1\.25 / 1\.5 ×** a **16 px** base, applied to the base only~~ Type base **16 px** × the OS font size (removed from the app 2026\-09\-25, schema 5) | §2.5 | `typeRamp()` |
| Spacing unit `u`: **8 px** comfortable, **6 px** compact; `pad` \= 1\.75u, `gap` \= 1u | §2.5 | `density` |
| Touch/click target minimum: **48 dp** Android, **40 px** Windows, at every density and scale | §2.5 | — |
| Hit\-area expansion for controls drawn smaller than the minimum: ~~**44 px**~~ **the minimum itself, 48 dp / 40 px** (corrected 2026\-09\-25) | §2.5 | — |
| Backup/store schema version: ~~**1**~~ ~~**3**~~ ~~**4**~~ ~~**5**~~ ~~**9**~~ **10** (10: a Profile's language, §2.7, 2026\-10\-26; corrected 2026\-09\-25, twice — the fix pass moved the build to 5 and left this row at 4; 1→2, 2→3, 3→4 and 4→5 above; **corrected again 2026\-10\-26: 6 the app lock, 7 the automatic copy, 8 the Trash, 9 hourly income — this row had stayed at 5**) | §12 (above) | `SCHEMA_VERSION` (the Room `version` and the backup file's field are this one constant) |
| Credit\-card minimum\-payment walk and payoff walk cap: **1,200 months** | §9.2 | `Accounts.payoffAtFixedPayment`, `payoffAtMinimum` |

**Sign convention:** `Transaction.amount` is the signed delta applied to the named target's own tracked quantity; the Savings movement per `key` is given in §3.6's table. Every ledger\-reading feature (§5.1, §5.2, §5.3, §5.4, §7.3) executes against that table.

**Derived, not stored.** Three figures are computed on read and hold no state: **`incomeReceivedThisMonth`** (§3.3 — the sum of `isIncome` Transactions dated in the current calendar month and on or after `incomeEnabledDate`; it appears in no entity row below, deliberately), and **`Goal.months`/`Goal.monthly`** (§6.1 — derived from `targetDate`, `target` and `saved`, and listed in Goal's row only for findability). Deriving the first is what keeps §3.9's triggers order\-independent; deriving the second is what stops a goal's deadline silently decaying.

**Icons:** `icon` on Goal, Category and RecurringExpense is nullable and never asked for at creation — §6.3's minimal path is Name \+ optional amount, and adding a required picker would contradict it. It is chosen from a fixed, app\-supplied set on the item's own editor screen. `null` renders a neutral per\-entity default glyph. §2.5's low\-distraction mode hides icons entirely, which is the other reason nothing may depend on one being set.

| Entity | Key fields | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Profile** | id, name | **Built in the prototype (§8's build, recorded here 2026\-09\-14); a backup file (§4.7) is the `profiles[]` array plus `schemaVersion`, `activeProfileId` and `exportedAt` — nothing else exists above the wrap.** Originally: Deferred (§8) — not built this pass, but the shape every other entity, **Transactions and Settings included in full**, should nest under eventually (§8.4). Entity ids must be unique across Profiles, or `key: goal:<id>` collides between them. **`kind` (self/dependent/joint/shared) removed 2026\-09\-02:** nothing read it, and its enum encoded a *whose\-money* taxonomy §8.1 explicitly rejects as the wrong test — §8.1's own example list includes an inheritance or trust fund, which fits none of the four values. What replaces it is a question for the Profiles phase (§13.2, moved there from §13.1 on 2026\-09\-02). |
| **Savings** | amount (**nullable**) | Exactly one per Profile (§3.1). Not an Account. **`null` is the fresh\-install state and a real third state distinct from zero (§3.1, §11.7)** — it suppresses §3.5's threshold, §5.1's figures, and §6.4's sustainability view when Income is off, and drives §11.7's message; the figure position renders an em dash, never "€0.00". A deliberate `0` is a real answer and behaves like any other value. **Any action that would move Savings ends the null state** rather than being blocked by it, treating the starting figure as €0 (§3.1). The first *positive* movement opens the ledger's first lot (§5.1); a zero opens none, since there is nothing to date. |
| **Goal** | id, name, icon (nullable), saved, target (nullable), **targetDate (nullable)**, mode (`date`\|`amount`, nullable), months (*derived*), monthly (*derived*) | `target: null` is the no\-target mode (§6.3) — saved accumulates, nothing is ever marked "achieved." **`targetDate` added 2026\-09\-02:** §6.1's by\-date mode and §6.3's "without an end date" both presuppose a stored date, but only `months` — a duration with no anchor — was stored, so a ten\-month goal stayed ten months out forever while its deadline silently slid, and every downstream figure in §6.2/§6.4 went stale with it. `targetDate: null` is §6.3's no\-date mode. **`mode`'s second value renamed `pace` → `amount` (2026\-09\-02)** to match §6.1's own "by an amount"; nothing connected `pace` to anything. All three are unset when there's nothing to compute — the test §6.4 applies is a null `monthly`. A Goal with `saved ≥ target` stops counting toward §6.2's other\-goal commitments but stays fully contributable\-to. Deleting one prompts to return `saved` to Savings (§3.6). |
| **Category** | id, name, icon (nullable), planned, spent, lastResetMonth | plan\-vs\-actual; `spent` directly editable and quick\-add\-incremented (§3.6); `lastResetMonth` is the shared cycle\-tracking field §3.9 needs. **Set to the creation month at creation** so a category created mid\-month isn't reset on its first app\-open. Deleting a Category returns nothing to Savings and writes no Transaction (§3.6). |
| **RecurringExpense** | id, name, icon (nullable), amount, cadence (`weekly`\|`biweekly`\|`semimonthly`\|`monthly`\|`bimonthly`\|`yearly`), **firstDueDate**, dueDate, dueDate2 (semimonthly only), **dueDateAnchorDay** (fixed\-date mode only), dateMode (`interval`\|`fixedDate` — **applies only to `monthly`, `bimonthly`, `yearly`**), **intervalDays** (interval mode only), **apportionedShare** (bimonthly/yearly only, nullable), reminderEnabled, reminderLeadDays (`1`\|`3`\|`7`\|`14`), ~~**lastReminderFiredFor (nullable)**~~ (removed, schema 4), lastSweptMonth, endDate (nullable), **finishedAt (nullable)**, **linkedAccountId (nullable)** | Automatic — swept against Savings on the New Month boundary (§3.11); each real occurrence writes its own dated Transaction, so the entity keeps no running total. `dueDate` is a **live, mutating anchor that advances when its due date passes in the real world** — from the start of the following day — **never when the sweep fires**; a past date entered at creation is normalised forward by its cadence. **`firstDueDate` added 2026\-09\-02:** because `dueDate` is normalised forward, counting a month's occurrences requires walking the chain backward, and without a floor that walk invents occurrences from before the expense existed — a loan whose first payment is deliberately one month after origination (§9.1) would otherwise sweep a phantom payment in its origination month. It is the entered, pre\-normalisation date and bounds every occurrence count, threshold figure and sweep. **`dueDateAnchorDay` added 2026\-09\-02:** in fixed\-date mode `dueDate` snaps to a short month's last day, and without a separate immutable anchor that snapped value became the new anchor, permanently relocating a 31st\-of\-the\-month rent to the 28th after one February. **`intervalDays` added 2026\-09\-02:** interval mode was defined only as "a fixed day\-count," which is not a number — 30 vs 31 changes whether a monthly interval yields twelve or thirteen occurrences a year and can put two in one calendar month. Defaults 30/60/365 for monthly/bimonthly/yearly, set once at creation. **`apportionedShare` added 2026\-09\-02:** §3.11 requires the long\-cadence share to be set per cycle and held; recomputing it monthly from a shrinking divisor would make it grow. Set at creation, when `dueDate` advances, and on any confirmed edit to `amount`, `cadence`, `dueDate` or `endDate` — and **only** at those moments, since recomputing it on any other read is the shrinking\-divisor growth §3.11 rejects. On an advance it is measured from the previous occurrence rather than from today, so a rollover the app was closed for yields the share that cycle should have had (§3.11, 2026\-09\-04). **`dateMode` no longer offered for `weekly`/`biweekly`** — both are inherently intervals. **`finishedAt` added 2026\-09\-02:** a terminal marker §3.9's catch\-up needs to know whether a multi\-month replay has already passed the final occurrence; it records that an event happened — the same role `lastSweptMonth` plays — and is not the occurrence counter §3.11 refuses. Set by `endDate` being passed, or by an auto\-updating Loan's balance reaching zero (§7.2). `dueDate` stops advancing once it is set. **`linkedAccountId` added 2026\-09\-02** so the Account pair is navigable in both directions (§7.2). All four creation branches set `lastSweptMonth` to the creation month (§3\.11). ~~**`lastReminderFiredFor` added 2026\-09\-04:** §3.11's due\-date reminder must fire once per occurrence, and nothing recorded that it had. It holds the `dueDate` it last fired for, so a reboot's re\-registration does not re\-fire what already fired and an advanced anchor re\-arms the reminder without a separate call — the same terminal\-marker role `lastSweptMonth` and `finishedAt` already play, not a second occurrence counter.~~ **Removed 2026\-09\-25 (schema 4):** nothing wrote it; the reminder's fire time is the guard (§3.11). |
| **Account** | id, name, type (`credit`\|`loan`), balance, **apr** (credit only, nullable), **linkedRecurringExpenseId (nullable, both types)**, autoUpdateFromPayments (loan only), **principal, interestRate, termMonths, rateType (`fixed`\|`variable`\|`interestOnly`), originationDate** (loan only, all nullable) | Informational and Savings\-vs\-Owed\-only (§7.1) — liabilities, both types; subtracted in Savings vs. Owed (§5.3). `checking`/`cash` removed (§7.1); Investment removed from the app entirely (§3.4). **The amortization inputs were added 2026\-09\-02** because §7.2's auto\-update decrement splits principal from interest and nothing stored the inputs — the feature was unimplementable as written, and any guess (decrementing by the full payment) understates the debt for the whole term and propagates into §5.3. The split **recomputes from live state each cycle** (`interest = balance × interestRate ÷ 12`) rather than from a table fixed at origination, so it survives extra payments, manual corrections and rate resets, and **floors at zero**, finishing the linked expense rather than driving the tracked debt negative. Storing the inputs also makes auto\-update reachable for a manually created loan, the dominant real case. **`apr` added 2026\-09\-02** so §9.2 Surface 1's ambient panel isn't blank on every visit. **`linkedRecurringExpenseId` extended to Credit 2026\-09\-02**, since §9.2's payoff panel can create a fixed\-payment expense; a Credit link carries no auto\-update, only the pairing, and deleting either side cascades with a prompt (§7.2). Creation with a non\-zero balance, deletion, quick\-add, direct edits, auto\-update decrements, **and §3.11's reconciliation difference on a Loan\-linked expense** all log a Transaction (`key: acct:<id>`) even though none touches Savings. Retyping clears the departing type's fields (§7.2). |
| **ImportState** | importedKeys (array), payeeRules (map), importReminderEnabled (default false), importReminderDay, lastImportDate | **Wrapped per Profile (corrected 2026\-09\-14 — the prototype had left it outside the wrap, so two Profiles shared one dedup ledger; §8.4).** **Added 2026\-09\-06 for §4.3's statement import.** `importedKeys` is the dedup ledger: for every imported row it holds the bank's `<FITID>` where the format supplies one *and* a synthetic date/amount/description key, and both are checked — one format's id does not identify the other's rows, so recording only the id would let a change of export format silently re\-import a whole period. `payeeRules` maps a normalised payee to a Category id and is written only when the person assigns one, so a Category is never guessed on first sight and never asked for twice (§2.6's automation finding). `importReminderEnabled` is **default false**, unlike §3.9's New Month toggle, because it asks for an action — auto\-deposit's precedent (§3.3), not New Month's. Nests under Profile with everything else once §8 is built (§8.4). |
| **Transaction** | id, date, loggedAt (real timestamp of creation, distinct from the editable `date`), payee (optional), amount (signed — see §3.6's table), key (`savings`, `goal:<id>`, `cat:<id>`, `rec:<id>`, `acct:<id>`, and `spend:<goalId>` since 2026\-10\-09, §3.6), targetLabel (snapshot of the target's name at logging time), isIncome (boolean, default false), hoursWorked/rate (optional, §3.10) | Written by every quick\-add, sweep occurrence, auto\-deposit, catch\-up entry, direct edit to Savings.amount / Goal.saved / Category.spent / Account.balance, Savings' first positive value, Account creation with a non\-zero balance, Account deletion (a closing row, §5.3), Loan auto\-update decrement, §3.11's edit\-and\-reconcile difference, accepted Goal\-deletion reversal, and accepted Recurring\-Expense\-deletion reversal — every path that moves a tracked figure is exactly as traceable as a quick\-add. Log/history only, never the source of truth for the field it touches (§3.6); `date` is editable at creation and governs ledger\-derived reports only, while the live field moves immediately. **Not editable, and not individually deletable — the one narrow exception being pair\-deletion** (§7.3): same `key`, equal amount, opposite sign, immediately following in `loggedAt` order, within 48 hours, evaluated once and never re\-derived, with sweep rows excluded entirely. Pair\-deleted rows are excluded from §5.1's and §5.3's replays. `targetLabel` exists so a later\-renamed or deleted target doesn't orphan its own history. `isIncome` is set automatically on every auto\-deposit and on any entry logged via Savings' dedicated Income "↓" button (§3.3, §3.6) — never inferred from amount or payee text. Nests under Profile once §8 is built (§8.4). |
| **Settings** | theme (default ~~`slate`~~ `swiss-blue`), mode (`system`\|`light`\|`dark`, default `system`), ~~scale (`sm`\|`md`\|`lg`\|`xl`, default `md`)~~ (removed from the app 2026\-09\-25, schema 5), density (`comfortable`\|`compact`, default `comfortable`), reduceMotion (default false), lowDistraction (default false), currency (default `EUR`), incomeEnabled (default false), income, incomeCadence (`weekly`\|`biweekly`\|`semimonthly`\|`monthly`), incomePayDate, incomePayDate2 (semimonthly only), incomeAutoDeposit, **incomeEnabledDate**, lastIncomeDepositDate, lastIncomeLoggedDate, newMonthNotificationEnabled (default true), notificationPermissionGranted, savingsVsOwedEnabled (default false), onboardingComplete, **goalPromptDismissed**, **catchUpSummaryPending**, **catchUpDepositCount, catchUpDepositTotal, catchUpMonths, catchUpSwept, catchUpEnded (schema 4, §3.9 — the stored card)**, **onboardingStep (nullable; schema 2, §11.1 — added to this row 2026\-09\-25)**, **trendMonths (default 6)** | **`trendMonths` added 2026\-09\-24 (§4.6, schema 3)** — the window §5.1's, §5.3's and §5.4's trends are computed over, which the person chooses. It is stored rather than held in view state, unlike which report is selected, because a window is a preference and a selection is a position. **Appearance defaults added 2026\-09\-02** — §2.5 now carries the concrete values, and §11.1's "whatever theme is already selected" resolves to Slate. `income` is a per\-paycheck amount, not a flat monthly figure (§3.3). **The full Income\-toggle\-off clearing set is `income`, `incomeAutoDeposit`, `incomePayDate`, `incomePayDate2`, `incomeEnabledDate`, `lastIncomeDepositDate`, `lastIncomeLoggedDate` — `lastIncomeDepositDate` added 2026\-09\-02.** That omission was the most dangerous single gap this pass found: it is the marker §3.9's auto\-deposit catch\-up compares against now, and catch\-up replays every elapsed cycle with real historical dates, so re\-enabling Income eight months later would have deposited \~17 paychecks the person never received into Savings as real, dated Transactions. It starts `null` when auto\-deposit is switched on, so the first deposit is the `incomePayDate` just supplied. **`incomeEnabledDate` added 2026\-09\-02**, doing double duty: the fallback reference point §3.3's "no income logged" check names but had nowhere to read, and the lower bound that makes the derived `incomeReceivedThisMonth` restart from zero on a mid\-month re\-enable. **`incomeReceivedThisMonth` and `lastIncomeResetMonth` are no longer stored (2026\-09\-02)** — see the derived\-figures note above. Changing `incomeCadence` while Income stays on re\-asks the anchor question and resets `lastIncomeDepositDate` (§3.3). `notificationPermissionGranted` is re\-read from the OS on app open — from the runtime dialog's answer on Android, from the system notification setting on Windows (§11.5) — and surfaced by the one line in Settings → Notifications (§4.7). **`goalPromptDismissed` added 2026\-09\-02** — §11.3's prompt is specified to appear "once," which needs a flag or it isn't once. **`catchUpSummaryPending` added 2026\-09\-02** — §3.9's summary would otherwise render only on the open that ran the catch\-up, so a person who backgrounds the app before reading it never learns that months of sweeps left Savings. `onboardingComplete` is one\-way (§11.6) **and per\-Profile (2026\-09\-14): each Profile is onboarded once, by §11.1's screen 1, and a Profile from a backup keeps its own value.** Nests under Profile in full once §8 is built (§8.4). **§3.10's four, added 2026\-10\-26 (schema 9):** `incomeType` (`salaried`\|`hourly`, default `salaried`), `hourlyRate` (default 0), `lastPaydayHandled` (nullable), `paydayNotificationEnabled` (default true). |
| **Shift** | id, date (the day worked), hours, rate (per hour) | **Added 2026\-10\-26 (§3.10, schema 9).** Logged hours not yet paid; moves nothing, and the payday that covers its date turns it into income once confirmed. Wrapped per Profile and carried by a backup like every other thing the person entered. |
