## 13\. Open Questions, Backlog & Decision Trail

**Restructured 2026\-09\-02.** This section previously ran a short numbered list into a changelog roughly nine thousand tokens long — around 40% of the whole document — which made the part anyone actually needs to act on the hardest part to find, and let a closing summary drift out of step with both. It is now four parts: what is genuinely unresolved (§13.1), what is deliberately postponed (§13.2), what is decided but unbuilt (§13.3), and the dated trail of how everything got decided (§13.4). Only §13.1 contains open design work.

### 13\.1 Live open design questions

~~**Nine open as of 2026\-09\-24, listed under *The 2026\-09\-24 set* below.**~~ ~~**Ten open as of 2026\-09\-25: the 2026\-09\-24 set below, and item 10.**~~ ~~**Seven open as of 2026\-09\-25, later the same day: items 1–6 and 9. Items 7, 8 and 10 were decided with the owner and are struck in place.**~~ ~~**Four open as of 2026\-09\-26: items 2, 3, 4 and 9.**~~ ~~**Three open as of 2026\-09\-26, later the same day: items 2, 4 and 9** (item 3 decided the same way, from a census and real\-app renders).~~ ~~**Two open as of 2026\-09\-26: items 4 and 9**~~ ~~**One open as of 2026\-10\-23: item 4**~~ **None open as of 2026\-10\-25** (item 4 built as Learn topics 8 and 9, §4.5) (item 9 resolved by §4.7's automatic copy; item 2 decided with the type system, §2.5). Items 1, 5 and 6 were scored on the real layout's measured sizes, shown to the owner as real\-app renders (`design/mockups-2026-09-26/`), and decided; they are struck in place. ~~None open as of 2026\-09\-14 (evening) — item 2 was resolved the day it was opened.~~ **That count held until the §4.8 desktop work reopened the section**; the item below it is the 2026\-09\-06 trail, kept because the reasoning is what its decision rests on.

**The 2026\-09\-24 set.** Each is a decision the specification does not make, found while implementing §4.8's column rule, §4.6's trend window and §12's migration path. None blocks the build — all are green at 251 tests — and each is named with what would settle it.

1. **Resolved 2026\-09\-26 — each keeps its phone\-360 shape (§2.1, with the owner).** ~~**Three components take their width from the container and their height from a `dp` literal**~~ (§2.1, §2.5). `components/Progress.kt:52` draws §2.1's bar at `height(5.dp)`, `ui/Charts.kt:37` the sparkline at `64.dp`, `ui/Charts.kt:71` the month columns at `150.dp`. Nothing in §2.5 gives a component a **height** on the screen axis — `u`, `pad`, `gap` and icons are there, radii, strokes and the ramp deliberately are not — so each author reached for a literal. This was harmless while rule 2 held the bar inside a 368dp pane at roughly 63:1; under the column rule it is **about 86:1 at the default text size and worse at XL**, and §2.1 says the hatch is load\-bearing information rather than decoration. Two narrower questions were put on 2026\-09\-24 and dismissed — whether the corner radius, hatch stroke and tick follow the bar's thickness (which would leave `radii.micro`'s fixed\-on\-every\-axis rule), and whether the sparkline and the month columns get the same treatment — so the shape of the fix is open, not only its value.
2. **Resolved 2026\-09\-26 — yes: the measure follows the device's text size, like body (§4.8, §2.5, with the owner).** ~~**Whether two columns being text\-size dependent is intended** (§4.8, §2.5).~~ The measure is `base × 0.55 × 52`, so it grows with §2.5's ramp: **458dp at MD, 686dp at XL**. Two columns need `2 × measure + gap`, which the default 1084dp window clears at MD and does not at XL. §4.8's column rule is correct either way — it is a maximum, and thinning to one column is the rule protecting density — but the section does not say that a person who raises their text size loses the second column, and that is a visible consequence nobody chose. **Premise changed, found 2026\-09\-26 while scoring items 1, 5 and 6:** since the in\-app text size was removed (§2.5, 2026\-09\-25), the measure (`SciFinTokens.measure`; its measureOf wrapper was removed 2026\-10\-10) reads the ramp's raw 16 and the OS font scale never reaches it, so the measure is a fixed 458dp at every text size — two columns no longer depend on text size at all, and at 150% text a measure\-wide line holds about 35 characters, not 52. The question is now whether the measure should follow the OS font scale again, which would bring back the dependence this item asked about.
3. **Resolved 2026\-09\-26 — the activity log, a reading pane, and the selected page (§4.8, with the owner).** ~~**What the second column holds on Activity, Learn and Settings** (§4.8).~~ Budget has Categories beside Envelopes and Reports has its reading material; the other three destinations are unscoped. A register\-scoring pass over `designkit`'s `register_fit` plus measured co\-visibility counts was proposed for them and not run.
4. **Resolved 2026\-10\-25 — two Learn topics, from fresh research (§4.5 topics 8 and 9, research audit §2.7 and §2.8, with the owner).** ~~**Reading material for §4.6's report views**~~ (§4.5, §4.6). Deferred by the owner on 2026\-09\-24 — *"Additional reading material will be scoped at a later point, starting from scientific research"* — and recorded in §4.5. Of §4.5's five topics only topic 1 and topic 4 map to a report at all; §5.1's Age of Money and §5.4's Income vs Expenses have none, which is why Income's supporting column currently holds a single note.
5. **Resolved 2026\-09\-26 — 88 (§4.1, with the owner).** ~~**The navigation rail's width: 88 or 240**~~ (§4.1, §4.8). ~~§4.1 fixes 88 today.~~ **The code fixes 88 today — `Frame.kt`'s rail, 11u; no section of this document states it (corrected 2026\-09\-25).** **None of the desktop applications surveyed in `design/desktop-prior-art-2026-09-23.md` uses an icon\-only rail**, and 160 was ruled out on text scaling. Changing it is a §4.1 amendment, and it also moves the centre of §4.8's column block, which is centred in the content area rather than the window.
6. **Resolved 2026\-09\-26 — outer, clamped to the work area (§4.8, with the owner).** ~~**The window's opening size**~~ (§4.8). `rememberWindowState(1100.dp, 760.dp)` sets AWT's **outer** bounds, so the client area is **1084 × 721**; 1116 × 799 would deliver 1100 × 760 of content. Which of the two the figure was meant to name has never been settled.
7. **Resolved 2026\-09\-25 — §12 rule 5.** ~~**What the two SQLite drivers do with a corrupt file** (§12).~~ No corruption handling is configured anywhere in the project — Room exposes an on-corruption hook and a keep-file-on-corruption factory, and a search for either, or for an open-or-recover path, returns nothing. The behaviour known from Room's Android *framework* helper — delete the file before anything can read it — may or may not apply to Room KMP's `AndroidSQLiteDriver` and `BundledSQLiteDriver`, and has never been checked here. It is the same family as the migration hazard — an open path that can destroy data before anything sees it — and §12's snapshot is what a fix would restore from. Deferred on 2026\-09\-24 until the migration work landed, which it now has.
8. **Resolved 2026\-09\-25 — §12 rule 5: one sheet, two answers.** ~~**Whether an unopenable database should have a surface** (§12, §4.7).~~ With every shipped version carrying a migration and §12's snapshot taken before the file is opened, the destructive fallback is no longer silent loss, so this is polish rather than safety. But nothing catches the open: Room's builder is lazy, the first real touch is `session.open()` inside `store.start()`, and on the desktop that runs **before `application {}`** — so an unforeseen gap would end the process before a window exists. A surface would need the open wrapped on both platforms, a screen outside the normal frame, and delete\-and\-recreate on confirmation before §4.7's restore.
9. **Resolved 2026\-10\-23 — by §4.7's automatic copy, with a caveat (owner).** The weekly copy writes an encrypted file to a folder the person chooses, so the artefact that can leave the device no longer depends on remembering to make one. It leaves the device only if that folder is cloud\-synced or on another drive, and the internal snapshots still do not survive an uninstall or Clear Data. ~~**Snapshots never leave the device**~~ (§12, §4.7). They are written beside the store, which on Android is app\-private: they survive an upgrade and not an uninstall or Clear Data. Prompting for a §4.7 export after an upgrade was offered on 2026\-09\-24 and not taken, so the artefact that can leave the device still only exists when someone remembers to make one. ~~One open as of 2026\-09\-14 — item 2, below.~~ ~~None open as of 2026\-09\-06.~~ The last item before it — category automation — was resolved that day and moved to §4.3; the trail of how it narrowed is kept below because the reasoning is what the decision rests on. One item again — the Settings question opened 2026\-09\-03 was resolved 2026\-09\-04 (§4.7). **Reduced from five to three, then two, then one, all on 2026\-09\-02** — the Profile taxonomy and the Overtime expected\-hours estimate moved to §13.2 as deferred rather than open; the cross\-platform stack was decided (§1); and the theme palettes were transcribed into §2.5 with the two failing token pairs remedied (§13.4). ~~What is left is the one question in this document with no mechanism proposed at all.~~ **That question was resolved 2026\-09\-06; what is open now is the 2026\-09\-24 set at the head of this section (corrected 2026\-09\-25).** Unbuilt\-but\-decided work is §13.3; deliberately postponed features and unspecified placeholders are §13.2.

10. **Resolved 2026\-09\-25 — they do not (§2.5).** ~~**Whether touch targets grow with the screen**~~ (§2.5, §12; opened 2026\-09\-25 by the audit, left open by the owner — *"layout design has been a complex issue in this project"*). §2.5 says it two ways: the 2026\-09\-23 phone rule says targets are *"unaffected, as they are by every other axis"*, and the touch\-target paragraph gives `48px × max(1, uiScale)`. The build uses a fixed 48dp on Android and 40px on Windows. Tied to it: the small\-control hit area is written as 44px in §2.5 and §12's table, below the stated 48dp floor, while the build's expansion uses the platform minimum. What settles it: a decision on whether a larger phone gets larger targets, then one sentence struck.

*The resolved items and the category\-automation trail that stood here moved verbatim to §13.4's 2026\-09\-05 → 09\-06 group on 2026\-09\-25 (decided with the owner), so this section holds only what is open.*

**A standing limitation, not an open question:** the credit\-score resource list (§9.3) has no mechanism for staying current — nothing re\-checks a government agency's URL or name after its entry's creation date. This is accepted and named rather than solvable, and needs periodic manual review once built. It is recorded here so it isn't mistaken for an oversight, but no decision is pending on it.

### 13\.2 Deferred by design

Not open questions — scheduled later phases, with their design settled to the depth each needs, plus two surfaces that are frankly placeholders. **Two entries arrived here from §13.1 on 2026\-09\-02** (the Profile taxonomy and the Overtime expected\-hours estimate): both are real unfinished thinking, but neither is answerable before its own phase begins, which makes them deferred rather than open. **The Windows desktop layout left this list the same day** — it was logged here as undesigned, and §4.8 has since designed it, so it now sits in §13.3 as decided\-but\-unbuilt.

- ~~**Profiles** (§8)~~ **Built 2026\-09\-14** (§8.4). Originally: the data model wraps everything, Transactions and Settings included in full, under a future `profiles: [...]` array (§8.4). No structural change this pass.
- ~~**Hourly income** (§3.10) — sketched, not scheduled.~~ **Built 2026\-10\-26 (§3.10, schema 9).** The Transaction metadata it needed (`hoursWorked`/`rate`) was already decided (§12).
- ~~**Overtime & Self\-Worth** (§10)~~ **Built 2026\-10\-23** (§10.3). Originally: scope, tone constraint and disproportionate\-impact framing all decided; content and calculator not built.
- ~~**Full transaction ledger screen** (§7.3)~~ **Built 2026\-09\-14** (`ui/screens/LedgerScreen.kt`, with pair deletion behind typed DELETE). **Two refinements (Decided, 2026\-09\-15):** the two filters are two collapsed lists side by side — *Show* and *Range* — instead of ten chips over two unlabelled groups that took the top 40 % of the screen; and a row dated after today reads *"· scheduled"* with its amount in `txt2`, because §3.11 books a sweep on its due date and the log was showing a booked sweep as a thing that had happened (`ledgerRowNote()`). Originally: architecture fully decided, including pair\-deletion's eligibility rules; the browse/filter/search surface itself not built. **Worth noting for sequencing:** §5.1 names pair\-deletion as the only remedy for a mistake\-and\-correction pair distorting Age of Money, so until this screen exists that remedy has no UI.
- **Profile taxonomy** (§12, §8.4 — moved here from §13.1 on 2026\-09\-02) — `Profile.kind`'s enum (self/dependent/joint/shared) was removed this pass because it encoded a *whose\-money* test §8.1 explicitly rejects; §8.1's own example list includes an inheritance or trust fund, which fits none of the four values. What, if anything, replaces it is a question for the Profiles phase and cannot be answered before it: nothing reads the field, and §8.1's corrected test — whether something external makes the money genuinely wrong to pool, not whose it is — is already stated in prose and needs no enum to be understood or applied. Listed here rather than as a live question because there is no decision available to make today.
- **Settled 2026\-10\-23 (owner): it does, only with Income on, defaulting to 160 and never saved.** ~~**Whether §10.2's Overtime calculator collects an expected\-hours estimate at all**~~ (§3.10, §10.2 — moved here from §13.1 on 2026\-09\-02) — §3.10 dropped its own justification for the estimate when §3.5 and §6.2 moved to real landed income, leaving §10.2's Salaried pre\-fill as its only remaining consumer. **It is not a live question, because §10.2 has already made it optional:** the rate field there is "always present and always editable" and the two income branches are "pre\-fills, not gates," so the calculator is fully specified and fully usable whether or not the estimate is ever collected. A nice\-to\-have inside a deferred feature, to be settled when §10 is picked up.
- ~~**Data & backup, and security & lock** (§4.7) — two Settings entries that are genuinely placeholders~~ **Data & backup was designed and built 2026\-09\-14 (§4.7); Security & lock 2026\-10\-20 (§4.7), so this entry is closed.** ~~Security & lock remains the one Settings entry that is a genuine placeholder: unbuilt *and* undesigned, with no section specifying it. Listed here so it isn't read as specified work waiting to be picked up. Whoever takes it on is designing from scratch — though a sibling project's shape (the platform's own biometric prompt with the device credential as fallback, no in\-app PIN to store or throttle, off by default) is the obvious starting point.~~

### 13\.3 Implementation backlog

~~All Decided, none built.~~ **Stage 1 of the native build landed 2026\-09\-14 (§14): every engine item below is built in shared Kotlin under `native/shared`, from this document, with 140 tests — one per dated rule (143 since the 2026\-09\-14 theme rename: §14's 142 engine tests plus its headless render of the eight palettes, which is how §14 counts the same run — reconciled 2026\-09\-25) — and a parity fixture that replays the prototype's seeded ledger and requires every report figure to match. Stage 2, the screens, landed the same evening in §14's measured order — 184 tests, every screen rendered headlessly over the parity fixture, the first run walked on hardware.** What each item's screen still lacks is marked. Grouped by what would sensibly be built together.

- ~~**The schema pass** (§12)~~ **Built 2026\-09\-14** (`model/Entities.kt`, `data/SciFinDatabase.kt`, schema 1 exported). Originally: §12 is Decided as to shape and completeness but not as to storage form: types, indexing, migration, and how the entities map onto a real store. Engineering work rather than a design question, but it comes first and it is not nothing.
- ~~**The New Month engine** (§3.9, §3.11)~~ **Built 2026\-09\-14** (`engine/NewMonth.kt`, `engine/Recurring.kt`; 35 tests, every §3.11 bullet named; the editor, the reconcile and reversal prompts and §3.9's card the same evening, `ui/screens/RecurringEditorScreen.kt` — §3.11's completion notices ride on that card as plain lines, an in\-app placement pending the person's say on whether they should be OS notifications instead). Originally: the shared 1st\-of\-month trigger and catch\-up, Category reset, the Recurring Expense sweep with its anchor chain, apportioned shares, `endDate` termination and deletion\-reversal math, plus auto\-deposit's separate payday\-cadence trigger. The largest single piece of work in the document, and the one most other behaviour depends on.
- ~~**Platform notification scheduling** (§3.9, §3.11, §11.5)~~ **Compiled and wired 2026\-09\-14** — the seam compiles on both targets, `engine/Reminders.kt` derives the registrations, `app/AppSession.kt` hands them over on every open, and the Android host installs the permission launcher. Originally: real OS\-level scheduling on both targets for the New Month notification *and* §3.11's per\-expense due\-date reminders, which share the subsystem and must be rescheduled whenever a `dueDate` advances. Permission handling where the platform has one, boot recovery. A genuine commitment, doubled by §1's platform scope. **The app side of this is now built and its seam is a single function (2026\-09\-04)** — what schedules, what re\-registers, what fires once per occurrence, and what the permission gates — so what remains here is the two native scheduling implementations behind the `expect`/`actual` seam §1 already commits to, not the logic that decides when a reminder is due.
- ~~**The Savings/Income cluster** (§3.1, §3.3, §3.6)~~ **Built 2026\-09\-14** (`engine/Income.kt`, `engine/Writes.kt`). Originally: nullable Savings and its unset state, the two quick\-add buttons and their direction controls, the Savings full editor, the derived `incomeReceivedThisMonth`, the "no income logged" check, and the full toggle\-off clearing set.
- ~~**The ledger** (§3.6, §7.3, §12)~~ **Built 2026\-09\-14** (`engine/Ledger.kt`; the replay of the prototype's 112 rows reaches its €3,067 exactly). Originally: Transaction writing on every path including opening and closing balances and auto\-update decrements, the sign convention, and pair\-deletion. Every Report is derived from this, so it comes early.
- ~~**The Reports engines** (§5.1, §5.2, §5.3, §5.4)~~ **Built 2026\-09\-14** (`engine/Reports.kt`; the four sub\-views and their Canvas charts the same evening, `ui/screens/ReportsScreen.kt`, `ui/Charts.kt`; Age of Money 59 days and its six\-point trend *(both corrected 2026\-09\-25: the build dropped an overshoot and read the trend same\-day, as the prototype does; with §5.1's deficit carry and month\-end as\-of rule the seeded ledger reads 42 and 13 · 28 · 27 · 44 · 45 · 42 — it dips to −€1,045, and the prototype's pool ends €1,045 above its own balance. The parity test now pins the spec's figures and the prototype's 59 as known drift)*, coverage 42 days, all eleven spending slices and the Savings\-vs\-Owed series match the prototype's). Originally: Age of Money's FIFO walk with its lot invariant and six\-month replay, the prospective toggle, Spending's netted composition, Savings vs. Owed's ledger\-derived backfill, and Income vs Expenses' conditional presence.
- ~~**The Goal calculator** (§6.1, §6.2, §6.3, §6.4)~~ **Built 2026\-09\-14** (`engine/Goals.kt`; the editor screen and its save/delete rules the same evening, `engine/GoalEditing.kt`, `ui/screens/GoalEditorScreen.kt`; §3.5's threshold note, which the prototype computed in its display code, is now the engine's `engine/Threshold.kt` with its own tests). Originally: the arithmetic, the completed\-goal exclusion, no\-target mode, and the two contextual views with their corrected income\-based threshold.
- ~~**Accounts** (§7.2)~~ **Built 2026\-09\-14** (`engine/Accounts.kt`; the Activity tab, the detail screen with §9.2's outlook, the editor, §7.3's ledger and §4.3's import screen the same evening — `ui/screens/ActivityScreens.kt`, `LedgerScreen.kt`, `ImportScreen.kt`, `engine/AccountEditing.kt`). - **§9's features** (§9.1, §9.2, §9.3) — **the maths and the two hand\-offs are built 2026\-09\-14** (`engine/Accounts.kt`: three rate types, both payoff walks, "Get loan" and "Save for it"); the calculator screens and §9.3's Learn topic are Stage 2. Originally: the Loan calculator with its three rate types, Credit Card's two surfaces, the Credit Score Learn topic and resource list.
- **Desktop layout** (§4.8) — **Stage 2, item 11 of 13 in the measured order (§14, 2026\-09\-14); built that evening — the rail, ~~list\-detail at 840dp~~ (withdrawn 2026\-09\-24 for §4.8's column rule), dialogs at 600dp, Reports single, the 52ch measure cap (`ui/Frame.kt`).** Originally: the width\-class navigation swap (bottom bar → rail), list\-detail two\-pane rendering against §4.2's *Pushes to* column, sheets becoming centred dialogs, Reports' single\-pane exception and the text measure cap. Decided 2026\-09\-02 and previously logged in §13.2 as undesigned. Sequencing note: the rules are cheap to honour if the navigation container and the detail\-push mechanism are built as width\-aware from the start, and expensive to retrofit afterwards, so this belongs with the first screens rather than after them.

Steps 2, 4 and 5 built later that evening (`ui/screens/OnboardingSteps.kt`; walked on the OnePlus through the OS dialog to Budget home), and the walk found the compiled Android seam reporting a fresh install as *denied* before it had asked — `areNotificationsEnabled()` is false until the runtime permission is granted, so it is now read second (§11.5). The tutorial, item 13, closed the stage that night (`app/Tutorial.kt`, `ui/TutorialOverlay.kt`: eight stops anchored to the real elements, Next driving navigation, one Skip, the replay from Settings; walked on the OnePlus from screen 1 through Done to the Currency landing).
