**Decided but unbuilt, as of 2026\-09\-24.** Four items, none of them open questions — each has its decision recorded and is waiting on the work.

- ~~**§4.5's four Learn body copies.**~~ **Done 2026\-10\-10 (§4.5, owner\-approved; this line corrected 2026\-10\-23).** The route was decided 2026\-09\-24 and is recorded in §4.5: the prototype's four bodies are adopted as the starting text and **each paragraph is checked against the audit section this list names for that topic**, with anything unsupported cut or rewritten. The blocker the 2026\-09\-14 note gave — the audit file not being in the project folder — is gone. Until the check is done the built screen still carries the titles and source lines with a one\-line body.
- ~~**`AppStore.kt:79`'s `twoPane` is dead code.**~~ **Done 2026\-09\-25: deleted, with `Frame.kt`'s unused `TabScreen`.** §4.8's rule 2 was withdrawn on 2026\-09\-24 and `Frame.kt`'s branch removed with it; the property now has exactly one reference in the tree, its own declaration.
- **The scale and dark pass — done 2026\-10\-26: every literal left is meant to be fixed.** Of the 16 counted below, 11 are §2.5's radii and strokes themselves, which are fixed dp by decision (2026\-09\-23); 2 are the icon vectors' nominal 24dp, matching their 24\-unit viewport — every icon is drawn at a scaling `t.icons` size, never at that one; and 3 are zeros (a padding, an offset and a floor), which no scale changes. The count stays, and `tools/speccheck.py` keeps failing the build on any new literal. ~~**95** (counted 2026\-09\-24)~~ ~~**62**~~ ~~**58**~~ ~~**44**~~ ~~**43**~~ **16 `N.dp` literals remain under `shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/app/scifin/ui/`** (in 5 files since the visual follow-up, 6 since the cleanup pass of 2026\-10\-10, 10 since the entry and placement mocks were removed on 2026\-10\-09, 11 before that and 13 before 2026\-10\-07; recounted by `tools/speccheck.py` on every run since 2026\-09\-25, after the icon sizes moved onto §2.5's tokens, and 58 since the three chart heights became shapes on 2026\-09\-26, and 44 since the two theme pickers became one token\-sized grid on 2026\-10\-07), against §2.5's rule that measurements scale with the screen; ~~three of them are item 1 of §13.1 above~~. ~~The pass also owes the **System** default for the text\-size setting.~~ ~~**It owes instead the setting's removal, decided 2026\-09\-23 (§2.5): `Settings.scale`, the text\-scale enum and the Appearance row (corrected 2026\-09\-25).**~~ **The setting's removal is built (2026\-09\-25, schema 5):** the field, the enum and the Appearance row are gone, and the three texts that named it were reworded with the owner.
- ~~**Whether Windows' text size reaches the desktop app** (§4.1, found 2026\-09\-26).~~ **It does not (checked 2026\-09\-26 in Compose Desktop 1.12's source); the host now reads `TextScaleFactor` itself (§2.5, built the same day).** The rail's landscape overflow at 150% was fixed the same day too (§4.1).
- ~~**Full localisation.**~~ ~~Every string is English in source.~~ **Built 2026\-10\-27 (§2.7), English and Italian, in three PRs; the background notifications' language fixed 2026\-10\-28.** Not yet walked on a device in Italian.

### 13\.4 Decision trail

- **2026\-10\-10 — the cleanup pass's last visual questions (owner).** The *Why …* links keep their 24dp tap height. The plan tick spans exactly the bar's height, with no overhang; the bar's 3dp top padding, which only existed to hold that overhang, is gone. A title with no Back button sits flush left. The goal editor's *Suggested* captions appear only once there is something to suggest. Import's Activity\-log link keeps the link style. Android reminders carry Budget's wallet glyph in the status bar (`ic_stat_scifin`), chosen on a score: candidates drawn by the app's renderer and compared with the icons in the phone's real status bar (`_cleanup-2026-10-10/score_status_icon.py`). The wallet won 87–91% of weightings; the two distinctness measures disagreed, so the choice rests on it being the app's own glyph. The desktop screen factor stays open (§2.5, 2026\-09\-23). Non\-visible: the scrim ink is a token, `SCRIM`, and the sparkline stroke is `strokes.line`.
- **2026\-10\-10 — the owner's answers to the cleanup pass's open questions.** Back during the tour steps to the previous stop, and before the first it ends the tour (§11.6). On Windows an unreadable notification state counts as on, Windows' default; only an explicit off is off (§4.7). Statement import: money in can stay in Savings (default) or go on into a Goal through Savings; a file that isn't a statement says so ("doesn't look like a bank statement … Nothing was imported") instead of "may have moved"; zero OFX rows are dropped as zero CSV rows are (§4.3). A damaged backup's refusal names the Profile and stops there, with the technical reason kept in the log (§10). Reports' Income vs Expenses counts Goal money once, when it is spent (§5.4). In the Activity log, a Goal spend reads *from \[Goal\]* where Account rows keep *tracked only*. A loan's completion notice is decided after that month's payment. *Loan finished* and *Account closed* stay distinct. The *Why …* links open their own topic. Learn's four bodies are written (§4.5). Country source addresses can be tapped. The Categories note lost its roadmap sentence. The floating \+ is announced as *Log*. The Savings row's glyph is a pencil. The date picker confirms with *Done*. Windows file dialogs are titled *Import a statement*, *Restore a backup* and *Save a backup*. A Goal saved with an empty target is a running tally. Spending exactly the plan reads *As planned*. The recurring row's total is exact, through the one formatter (§3.11); with none it reads *No recurring expenses yet*. Security & lock is hidden until app lock exists. Visible text uses curly quotes. Smaller fixes: the Accounts sentence has no serial comma and says *Choose one*; the calculators' goal defaults to *Savings goal*; *Create both* is primary and *Reset* sits at its natural width; Reports lost a tripled gap; every section label has Budget's spacing; another Profile's row is announced *Switch to \[name\]*. Money keeps one format, *€12.40*, everywhere.
- **2026\-10\-10 — a whole\-code cleanup pass (register: `_cleanup-2026-10-10/`).** Seven reviewers read the app code in small batches and every finding was checked against the code before it was fixed; 372 tests, all green. The behaviour it changed: statement import reads split *Debit/Credit Amount* columns, trailing minus signs, line breaks inside quoted fields and accented payees, and matches recurring names on whole words (§4.3); yearly and bimonthly expenses stop sweeping after their end date, and a loan's principal drops once per month even if the month is replayed (§3.9, §3.11); deleting an income pair no longer rewinds the income markers, and a backdated income no longer moves them back (§3.3); a backup with an unreadable transaction key or a repeated id is refused as damaged (§10); Back does nothing on onboarding's first screen, while Reconcile is open, or during the tour, and Settings' Back origin resets when the Profile changes (§4.1); on desktop Esc closes any sheet Back closes; an editor's Save waits until every typed figure reads; typed drafts survive an Android rotation; Windows reminders quote every value they pass to PowerShell and one bad reminder no longer stops the rest; an Android reminder opens the app when tapped, and two reminders with the same name no longer replace each other; the phone bar's top edge is the 1.5dp control stroke (§2.5). Dated history was taken out of code comments; it lives here.
- **2026\-10\-10 — the entry walked on the OnePlus 6T, and Settings moved off the phone's bar (§4.1).** The walk found Back never closing a sheet, the floating \+ over the goal prompt, and a doubled placeholder; all three fixed (§3.6).
- **2026\-10\-09 — one entry sentence for every editable element (§3.6), Savings without Money out, Goals spent from, import targets (§4.3), and the structure pass (§4.2, §11.6).** The entry, its wording, Done, the floating \+ and Ctrl\+N are in §3.6; the import rule in §4.3; Budget's order (M3, Goals second), the Activity note's sheet and the tutorial's split in §4.2 and §11.6. Scored in `design/audit-2026-09-25.md`.
- **2026\-09\-24 — §4.8's column rule, §4.6's trend window, and §12's migration path.** §4.8's rule 2 was withdrawn on rendered evidence (a fixed 368dp list beside 627–1447dp of void), replaced by **columns of one measure, centred**, with the second column holding another view of the same destination and never another destination. §4.3's segmented control became a narrow\-width affordance and the recurring summary unwraps at two columns. §4.8's Reports width clause was withdrawn on measurement — every trend is six fixed points, so width buys gaps rather than resolution — and Reports took a supporting column instead, scored across 1771 weightings rather than argued. §12's phased migration policy switched over **because its own condition expired**: destructive changes were admissible *while nothing real was stored*, which stopped being true at the 2026\-09\-23 device walks. §12 now carries `Migration(1, 2)`, `Migration(2, 3)`, a guard test, and a pre\-open snapshot written with `VACUUM INTO`. §4.6's trend window became the person's choice at 3, 6, 12 or 24 months. Three corrections are recorded where they belong: §4.8's own argument had been quoted backwards into it on 2026\-09\-24 and is fixed; §12's snapshot was specified reading the file header and had to read through SQLite instead, because an un\-checkpointed write\-ahead log leaves byte 60 stale; and §5.1's *"six months ago"* caption was one month wrong at every window and is now computed.

**Condensed 2026\-09\-02.** Each entry now names the decision, its date, and where it lives — the reasoning itself has been moved into the sections it governs, where an implementer will actually meet it, rather than being restated at length here. The uncondensed trail remains in this document's pre\-2026\-09\-02 revision if the longer form of any entry is ever wanted.

**Foundational (2026\-07\-18 → 07\-20)**

- Reports' default sub\-view → Buffer; Activity promoted to its own top\-level tab; per\-screen register audit (§4.1, §4.2, §5.1).
- Savings/Account merge scoped: exactly one Savings figure per Profile, Accounts informational\-only (§3.1, §7.1).
- Full Account CRUD; quick\-add fully specified and wired (§7.2, §3.6).
- Income toggle behaviour: hidden when off, cleared not remembered on toggle\-off (§3.3).
- Income corrected from a flat monthly figure to amount \+ cadence, after checking real pay\-frequency practice — U.S. BLS data shows biweekly (43%) and weekly (27%) far outweigh monthly, while most of Europe pays monthly (§3.3).
- Income → Savings resolved: an auto\-deposit toggle, off by default, plus a quick\-add pre\-fill on the manual path; anchor date asked as a plain fact question rather than a picker (§3.3).
- **The monthly cycle resolved, and the check\-in\-day picker retired for good (§3.9, §11.4).** One shared "this month" definition; Categories reset `spent` monthly while `planned` carries forward; missed cycles caught up retroactively with real historical dates rather than silently skipped. The onboarding day\-picker is dropped outright and replaced by an unconditional **New Month** notification on the 1st — riding the calendar boundary Categories already needed rather than requiring its own onboarding step. This is the decision §13.3 now calls the largest single piece of work in the document, and the one §4.5's dropped Learn topic ("Why a specific check\-in day helps") was left over from.
- Onboarding gap resolved: goal\-naming moved to a post\-Savings prompt (§11).
- Goal calculator criteria resolved: universal gate\-free math plus two independent contextual views; no\-target mode extended to cover no\-end\-date (§6.3, §6.4).
- Loan resolved: "Get loan" creates a real Account \+ Recurring Expense atomically, but the screen's default emphasis stays permanently savings\-oriented (§9.1).
- Credit Cards resolved as two surfaces — an ambient payoff panel on the Account, a pre\-decision purchase calculator from Learn (§9.2).
- Credit Score resolved as hybrid education with no computed number, plus a dated, government\-sources\-only country resource list (§9.3).
- First cold read (self\-simulated): fixed a dangling cross\-reference, clarified that the recurring\-threshold and goal\-feasibility notes can co\-occur, restored Buffer's retrospective/prospective distinction *(superseded 2026\-09\-02 — §5.1's headline is a live figure, and the retrospective claim is struck)*, stated that Savings can go negative, and surfaced the recurring\-cadence gap.

**Recurring Expenses redesigned (2026\-07\-21 → 07\-23)**

- **Automatic monthly sweep replaces manual logging** (§3.11). Cadence enum grown to six values; a due date required at creation; two date modes; real occurrence counts for short cadences and even apportionment for long ones; §3.5 and §6.2 read the swept figure directly; per\-item reminders with preset lead times; deletion\-reversal prompt. Removed from quick\-add's surface list.
- Scope boundary made explicit: fixed\-amount obligations only, variable\-amount recurring costs belong in Categories (§3.11).
- Termination added (`endDate`) so a Loan payment stops sweeping when the loan is paid off (§3.11, §9.1).
- Rationale reframed from a due\-date argument to a committed\-funds one: "this money was never available for anything else in the first place" (§3.11).
- Investment removed from the app entirely; §3.4's number retired in place rather than renumbering (§3.4).
- Accounts trimmed to Credit and Loan only — Checking and Cash were structurally redundant with Savings or Profiles and kept a real double\-counting risk alive (§7.1).
- Net Worth's sign convention fixed (Savings *minus* Account balances — the prior formula would have shown taking on debt as increasing the total) and demoted to an off\-by\-default toggle (§5.3).
- Semimonthly Recurring Expenses gained `dueDate2`; `dateMode` marked non\-applicable to semimonthly (§3.11).
- Loan Accounts gained `autoUpdateFromPayments` (§7.2).
- **Income\-vs\-expense smoothing asymmetry reversed** (§3.3, §3.5, §6.2): `incomeReceivedThisMonth` replaces `monthlyEquivalent()` everywhere a check reads against Savings, since a Savings\-availability check crediting an unlanded paycheck is the same error as crediting an expense that hasn't come due. `monthlyEquivalent()` survives, narrowed to §10.2.
- Zero/negative Savings scoped across §3.5, §5.1 and §6.4 — thresholds correctly fire unconditionally, the prospective toggle floors at 0 days, and §6.4's three suggestions are suppressed in favour of a plain\-fact line.
- `Goal.mode`/`months`/`monthly` marked explicitly nullable (§12).
- Transaction ledger architecture resolved as a pure append\-only log with no CRUD of its own, replacing an over\-engineered editable\-ledger draft. SMS auto\-detection considered and set aside (§7.3).
- Profiles' data\-model scope corrected: Settings nests under Profile in full, one wrap point rather than a device/profile split (§8.4).
- Credit\-score and Overtime topics gained their disproportionate\-impact framing, both stated as structural circumstance rather than trait claims (§9.3, §10.1, §10.3).

**Onboarding redesigned (2026\-07\-24 → 07\-29)**

- The 4\-step placeholder replaced by a real flow: Welcome\+Theme → notification permission → live\-overlay tutorial → Settings landing on Currency → Budget home (§11.1).
- Savings entry removed from onboarding entirely — discovered, not collected (§11.7).
- Tutorial specified as a live overlay on the running app rather than a scripted mock sequence, with one persistent Skip button (§11.6).
- §8.1's Profile test corrected while drafting the tutorial's Profiles copy: the real test is whether something external makes pooling the money genuinely wrong, not whose money it is (§8.1).
- Categories tutorial copy finalized (2026\-07\-29), closing the last item from the onboarding pass (§11.6, stop 4).

**Fresh\-context review (2026\-07\-25)**

- Stale Learn topic dropped — "Why a specific check\-in day helps" survived the retirement of the concept it taught (§4.5).
- **Direct\-edit\-to\-Savings linkage resolved** (§3.2, §3.6): an edit through a full editor screen is a second entry point to the same mechanism quick\-add uses, moving Savings by the exact inverse and logging a Transaction. Account.balance stays the one exception.
- Self\-caught follow\-up the same day: that rule needed an explicit carve\-out for Categories' automated monthly reset, which would otherwise have credited a whole month's spending back to Savings (§3.9).
- "No income logged in the last X days" added as the manual path's safety net (§3.3).
- Age of Money's calculation defined via FIFO lot\-consumption, generalized to work without Income enabled (§5.1).

**Pre\-implementation mechanics (2026\-07\-28)**

- Auto\-deposit's monthly cadence confirmed calendar\-day anchored with month\-end snapping, not a flat \+30 days (§3.3).
- The New Month engine and auto\-deposit's catch\-up confirmed as two separate triggers sharing one technique (§3.9).
- Catch\-up granularity specified: sweeps and deposits iterate per cycle, Category resets jump straight to current (§3.9).
- Savings' Income checkbox replaced by two separate quick\-add buttons; Savings gained its own full editor behind a typed\-UPDATE confirmation (§3.6).
- `incomeReceivedThisMonth` confirmed to clear on Income toggle\-off — it tracked a projection, not a balance (§3.3).
- Transaction pair\-deletion added as a narrow exception to immutability, with `loggedAt` added to close a backdating loophole (§7.3, §12).
- **"Net Worth" renamed "Savings vs. Owed"** — math unchanged, but the name carried a verdict the app's register works against everywhere else. Its history confirmed ledger\-derived (§5.3).
- A cross\-language `bimonthly`/`semimonthly` mix\-up surfaced and was corrected before reaching the document — worth noting because the two mean opposite things here and swapping them in code would be a real, hard\-to\-notice bug.

**Country coverage researched (2026\-08\-04)**

- Six credit\-score resource entries confirmed against official government sources (§9.3). Germany's correct source is the federal data\-protection authority, not BaFin, which explicitly does not supervise credit bureaus — catching this kept a plausible\-but\-wrong source out of the list.

**Pre\-implementation audit (2026\-09\-02 — this pass)**

A six\-part parallel audit against §14's build\-from\-spec bar, resolving fourteen genuine design forks and roughly sixty deducible defects. The substantive decisions:

- **Platform scope stated as Android and Windows 10/11** (§1), correcting a document that said Android in §1 and reasoned cross\-platform in §7.3.
- **Goal money is out of every Savings\-facing figure, deliberately** (§3.2) — it already has a purpose, so it is treated as spent; the conservative reading is preferred to one that would flatter the person's available room.
- **`lastIncomeDepositDate` now clears on Income toggle\-off** (§3.3, §12) — the most dangerous single gap found, since without it a re\-enabled auto\-deposit would deposit months of paychecks that never happened.
- **`Transaction.amount`'s sign convention stated once, as a table** (§3.6) — previously unstated, and readable two ways that invert every report.
- **Opening\-balance Transactions added** for Account creation and Savings' first value (§5.3, §12), without which §5.3's promised backfill was not derivable at all.
- **§3.11's `dueDate` anchor advances when the real due date passes, never when the sweep fires** (§3.11) — two live bullets previously specified two different clocks, and every deletion\-reversal amount depended on which an engineer picked. `dueDateAnchorDay` added so fixed\-date anchors stop drifting off the person's chosen day.
- **Age of Money's "retrospective" sentence struck** (§5.1) — it contradicted the FIFO definition added three months later. The lot\-pool invariant, deficit carry, trend as\-of rule and prospective window all specified.
- **§6.4's sustainability threshold rebased to income when Income is on** (§6.4) — it was the last Savings\-facing check still keyed to the toggle rather than to money that had actually landed.
- **Goal and Category deletion specified** (§3.6) — a Goal offers its earmarked money back, a Category returns nothing, for the same reason §3.9 exempts the monthly reset.
- **Loan amortization inputs, `apr`, `targetDate`, `finishedAt`, `linkedAccountId`, `incomeEnabledDate` and `goalPromptDismissed` added** (§12) — each required by a Decided mechanic that had nowhere to read or write it.
- **All three loan rate types create a Recurring Expense** (§9.1), on a refined §3.11 scope test — "constant between discrete, knowable changes" rather than "constant forever" — with interest\-only getting its own completion copy since "paid off" would be false.
- **Credit Cards: a chosen fixed payment may create a Recurring Expense, a minimum payment never can** (§9.2) — the latter varies every cycle and was never in §3.11's scope.
- **Income vs Expenses hidden when Income is off** (§4.6, §5.4) — a birthday gift is Savings, not income, and a permanent flat zero line is the hole §3.3 forbids.
- **Germany reclassified as scored** (§9.3), on a stated criterion — whether a person can obtain a score about themselves and act on it — since Canada and Germany are structurally identical and were filed in opposite buckets.
- **Profile switcher hook fixed at Settings → Other** (§4.7, §8.3), and the tutorial's Profiles stop dropped until §8 is built (§11.6) — a live\-overlay tour cannot teach an inert control.
- **Tutorial stop 3's copy corrected** (§11.6) — it stated the sweep mechanic wrongly in both directions, teaching the wrong model of the app's most surprising behaviour in the one place built to prevent surprise.
- **§2.1's tick\+hatch bar and §2.5's accessibility settings given real values** (§2.1, §2.5) — both were Decided sections whose only concrete source was the prototype.
- **Pair\-deletion's eligibility pinned** (§7.3) — ordering key, forward\-only pairing, no cascade, and the full scope of "recorded effect" including `incomeReceivedThisMonth`.
- Plus the mechanical sweep: a wrong §6.4\-for\-§6.2 pointer, a surviving "checkbox" reference, two live "Net Worth" usages, "Tracking account," §3.11's "already built," §12's "`investment` was never an Account type" contradiction, and unprefixed audit citations in §2.4.

**Prototype cross\-check and stack decision (2026\-09\-02 — same day, later pass)**

- **§13.1 reduced from five live items to two** — the Profile taxonomy and the Overtime expected\-hours estimate moved to §13.2 as deferred rather than open, the cross\-platform stack was decided, and category automation was narrowed to external ingestion only (§4.3, §13.1, §13.2).
- **§2.5's palettes found to carry ten tokens per mode, not six — and no hatch\-stroke token at all** (§2.5) — the hatch is derived from the accent rather than specified per palette, and the earlier six\-value list would have dropped the second surface level and the on\-accent colour.
- **The palette item reclassified from mechanical to a real design decision** (§2.5, §13.1) — measuring all eight palettes found `txt3`/`bg` at 2.44:1 – 3.43:1 and `bord`/`surf` at 1.27:1 – 1.56:1, failing their requirement in every palette, with `txt3` carrying the over\-reference tick mark that §2.1 and §2.5 both name as the app's only non\-color encoding of state.
- **The Windows desktop layout logged as undesigned, then decided the same day** (§1, §4.1, §4.8, §13.3) — §1 committed to Windows as a first\-class target while no section said what the app looked like there. **Resolved as adaptive reflow with the transform specified** (§4.8), on the ground that a separate desktop shell's only real payoff is density, and §4.2 already rejects density on the app's most\-visited screen as the status\-board failure §2.2 and §2.4 exist to prevent. Three rules — width\-class navigation swap, §4.2's *Pushes to* column as the two\-pane contract, sheets to dialogs — plus Reports as a single\-pane exception and a text measure cap. Moved from §13.2 to §13.3.
- **Cross\-platform stack decided: Compose Multiplatform** (§1) — chosen over Flutter, two native codebases, .NET MAUI and Capacitor/Electron on a weighted evaluation of seven criteria, six counted structurally (shared logic, UI surfaces, native seams, shipped runtimes, form\-factor coverage, language count) and one judged (the risk of failing §2.5's accessibility surface or §3.9's scheduling on either target). Margins: Compose Multiplatform 0.898, Flutter 0.873, .NET MAUI 0.798, Capacitor/Electron 0.788 at its best, two native codebases 0.502. **The eliminations are robust and the top is narrow** — two native codebases lose decisively on writing every engine twice, MAUI on having no density or text\-scale primitive to build §2.5 from, Capacitor/Electron on shipping two runtimes behind a bridge; but Compose and Flutter are close enough that re\-weighting toward binary size reverses them, which is why §1 records the runner\-up rather than only the winner. The judged criterion carried 30% of the weight and is reasoning rather than measurement — the honest limit of the exercise.
- **The `txt3` and `bord` contrast failures remedied, and all eight palettes transcribed** (§2.1, §2.5, §13.1) — `txt3` rescoped to decorative and inactive use only, under a stated WCAG exemption, with every informational use promoted to `txt2`, which already cleared 4.5:1 everywhere; the over\-reference tick moved out of `txt3` into `txt` and widened to 2px, clearing 9.89:1 – 12.63:1 against its track; and `bord` split into a decorative `bord` and a new `bordStrong` meeting ≥3:1 against both `surf` and `bg`, rather than darkening every boundary and turning the app into a wireframe. The token set went from a stated six to an actual eleven. **This closed the last build\-from\-spec exception (§14)** — the prototype is no longer needed to build anything.

**Register repair and navigation depth (2026\-09\-03 — same day, later pass)**

- **§2.3's `feasibility-note` found drawn at maximum emphasis, not reduced** (§2.3, §14) — a solid accent plate identical to the primary button, which made an ambient note the heaviest element on the app's home screen. Repaired to an accent wash, along with two ambient notes carrying closing language §2.3 forbids them and §3.5's threshold reading the swept figure rather than the scheduled one.
- **§3.5 gained §6.4's plain\-fact rule for a zero base** (§3.5) — the trigger is unchanged; only the copy changes, and only where the base is ≤ 0. The sentence it produced before took a ratio against nothing.
- **§3.5's note collapsed by default, and "permanently present" scoped** (§2.3) — the figures stay on screen, the interpretation goes behind a disclosure. Applies to the ambient kind only; §6.4's frame view stays expanded by its own rule.
- **§3.11's apportioned share put on its own formula** (§3.11, §12, §14 — 2026\-09\-04) — `amount ÷ sweeps remaining before the due date`, held for the cycle, recomputed only when the anchor advances and measured from the previous occurrence so a late catch\-up is not penalised. A divisor floor of 1 is recorded for the case the section does not cover. The share is now stated in the editor before it is committed to, since a mid\-cycle expense reserving at three times its average monthly cost is correct but should not be a surprise (§2.3).
- **§3.11's per\-occurrence Transaction rows built, and the reference device corrected** (§2.5, §3.11, §14 — 2026\-09\-04) — one dated row per real occurrence, the ledger\-derived reversal that depended on it, and the reconcile row moved onto the occurrence it adjusts. Separately, §2.5 gained a navigation\-label token at 0\.75× (Material `label-medium`) and the note that 360dp is the compact floor to test against rather than the device to design on: the prototype had been previewing a Material\-accurate type ramp on a screen a size\-and\-a\-half smaller than any current handset, which reads as oversized type.
- **§3.11's creation branches and due\-date reminders built** (§3.11, §12, §13.3 — 2026\-09\-04) — all four branches, the shared occurrence walk they needed underneath them, and the reminder subsystem up to the platform seam. Four defects came out of building rather than reading: a constant\-table occurrence count that left `firstDueDate`/`endDate`/`dueDate2` partly unread, colliding entity ids from a bare `Date.now()`, an anchor chain that never ran at app open, and the `weekly` \+ `fixedDate` state this document already called illegal being two taps away in the editor. One genuine spec ambiguity is now recorded rather than left open: a first due date in an *earlier* month matches none of the four named branches, and is folded into branch 2.
- **§4.8 built, and §3.9/§3.11's engine rebuilt** (§3.9, §3.11, §4.8, §14 — 2026\-09\-04) — the three items §14 had been carrying as deliberately stale are closed. Two defects were found by building rather than by reading: the month engine skipped every month but the newest across an absence, and `addMonths` on 31 January rolls into March, which would have made §12's `dueDateAnchorDay` snap to the wrong month entirely.
- **Text\-scale layout audited across all four settings** (§2.5, 2026\-09\-04) — every screen at every setting, checked for text ellipsised away or crossing its own card. Clean at the default; six defects at xl and two at every setting, all one class: horizontal rows that had no reflow rule. Produced §2.5's three layout rules and the stated bar.
- **§4.7 restructured as a hub; Appearance pushed to a spoke the next day** (§4.2, §4.7, §11.1, §11.6, §13.1) — decided against a flat column on measurement rather than taste: 2.9 screens at the default text scale and 4.0 at xl, on the one screen whose length no content design can reduce. Assessed via `app-register-layout`, the same skill §2.4 and §4.2 were originally derived from, which returned hub\-and\-spoke at depth 2 with primary emphasis on Appearance. **The same run found no argument for reordering any content screen** — identical fitness with §3.5's note in position 2 and position 5 — so nothing else moved.

**Geometry absorbed and the prototype audited (2026\-09\-03)**

- **§2.5's geometry system specified as proportions, not pixel lists** (§2.5, §4.8, §14) — the type ramp, spacing, radii, strokes and target floor all moved into the document, expressed as ratios off three independent scaling axes (screen, density, text scale). Colour had been the only part of the visual system this document carried values for; everything with a size in it still lived only in the prototype, which is the same build\-from\-spec gap the palettes had. The prototype's seven\-token ramp was adopted and **rebased from its 13px base onto §2.5's 16px** — its scale steps had been hand\-tuned rather than multiplied, running 0.909–0.944× where the document asks for 0.875× and topping out near 1.36× where it asks for 1.5×. Comfortable `gap` moved 10px → 8px so the one stated spacing unit actually produces the values derived from it, and the prototype's eleven distinct corner radii were rationalized to seven, four of them having sat 1–2px apart doing a single job.
- **The prototype's ledger sign convention found inverted** (§3.6, §12, §14) — it stored the *Savings effect* rather than the delta to the named target, the exact reading §3.6's table was written to forbid. This was not on any drift list and was found only by reading the file. It is the reason §14's list is now audited against the file rather than carried forward.
- **§14's drift list rewritten against the file, and found wrong in both directions** (§14) — five items it listed as stale had been implemented for some time, §7.3 was listed as drift despite being a Deferred section with no surface in either document or prototype, and two real defects went unlisted. A drift list that overstates is not merely untidy: it sends a reader to rewrite working code and hides what actually needs doing.

* * *

**Category automation, scored and resolved (2026\-09\-05 → 09\-06) — moved here from §13.1 on 2026\-09\-25, verbatim**

~~1. **Category automation from external data** (§4.3)~~ **— Resolved 2026\-09\-06: statement import, built. The mechanism is specified in §4.3; what follows is the trail.** Originally: ingesting real transaction or bank data from outside the app to populate a Category's `spent`. No mechanism decided, no timeline. **Scope narrowed 2026\-09\-02 (prototype cross\-check):** this item previously read "deriving `spent` from real transaction or bank data," which conflated two different things. Deriving `spent` from the app's *own* ledger is not open — §12 settles it, and settles it the other way: Transactions are log/history only and never the source of truth for the field they touch (§3.6), so `spent` is a stored, directly\-editable figure that quick\-add increments. Only external ingestion is open. Note that §7.3 has already closed off the obvious route for this app's platforms (SMS/notification scanning), so this needs a different approach entirely rather than a revival of that one.

**Option space enumerated and scored, 2026\-09\-05 — the question is narrower, and still open.** No mechanism is decided here; what follows records what was ruled out, what was measured, and which positions survive, so the eventual decision is made against evidence rather than from scratch. Seven candidates were scored on three objectives — **coverage** (share of category spending captured without manual entry), **cost to own** (build plus run plus maintain), and **posture preserved** (whether §8.3's "local, single\-owner data, not an online multi\-tenant system" still describes the app) — deliberately as an unweighted trade\-off front rather than a single score. **The weighting is the decision itself**\: it encodes how much privacy posture is worth against automation, which is a product judgement, not an arithmetic one.

- **The posture axis is binary, and it is the real structure.** Three candidates leave everything on the device (status quo, file import, notification listening) and two do not (an open\-banking aggregator; bank alert emails parsed by a cloud LLM). There is no gradient between them. So this is two decisions and the second only exists if the first goes one way: *does anything cross the device boundary*, and then a cost\-versus\-coverage trade inside whichever group that answer selects.
- **Coverage, by contrast, has no threshold** — the candidates run 0.00 / 0.55 / 0.60 / 0.70 / 0.75 / 0.80 / 0.90 with no gap anywhere. It is a smooth dial, so it cannot be the axis the decision turns on.
- **Notification listening was scored and then dominated.** It is a real option — see §7.3's corrected basis — but a Settings\-toggled periodic reminder to import (next bullet) beat it on coverage *and* on cost at identical posture, which removes it from the front outright rather than merely ranking it lower.

**The strongest on\-device position: file import plus an opt\-in reminder to do it (added 2026\-09\-05).** A CSV/OFX export the person takes from their bank, parsed on\-device, **plus a Settings toggle that periodically notifies them that it is time**. It attacks the precise reason plain file import scores poorly — the export is person\-initiated and therefore forgotten — without adding a second ingestion mechanism at all.

- **Its marginal cost is close to zero, because the subsystem already exists.** §13.3's notification\-scheduling item is built: `NotificationScheduler`'s `expect`/`actual` seam, both native implementations, and the declarative whole\-schedule `syncReminders` contract. "Remind me to import" is one more registration and one more toggle beside §3.9's New Month one. It needs **no sensitive permission, no MSIX capability, no per\-sender parsing corpus, and carries no store\-review risk** — which is what separates it from notification listening, whose cost is dominated by exactly those four.
- **Default off, per §2.3.** It asks for an action, so it follows auto\-deposit's precedent (§3.3) rather than the New Month notification's: nothing is switched on without a conscious choice. Once enabled it is the automation the person asked for rather than a nag, the same reading that makes auto\-deposit acceptable.
- **Duplicate imports are resolved exactly, not heuristically.** OFX carries `<FITID>`, a per\-account unique transaction id, so re\-importing an overlapping date range identifies every duplicate with certainty. Plain CSV varies; where a bank supplies no reference column the fallback is `(date, amount, description)` plus a stored high\-water mark. **This is materially easier than the alternative it replaces** — matching a notification against a statement row means reconciling two sources that will not spell the merchant the same way, whereas two imports are the same source in the same format.
- **It is batch, and that is its real limit.** Nothing is captured between imports. That is the whole of its coverage gap against a combined approach, and it is the trade to weigh.

**A register argument, not only a cost one (2026\-09\-05).** §2.6's automation finding holds that "every repeated decision a budgeting tool demands is a place people drop out, and the highest\-leverage fix is to stop asking rather than to ask more nicely." Continuous notification parsing produces a *stream* of small confirmations to accept or decline — more decisions, not fewer, presented as automation. A once\-a\-month prompt the person opted into is the shape that finding actually recommends. This is why the reminder position is favoured on grounds independent of its score.

**Whatever mechanism is eventually chosen, it produces a suggestion, never a write (Decided, 2026\-09\-05).** A parser was built and tested against a corpus of realistic bank and wallet notification text. On a tuned corpus it reached 12/12 with no wrong figures; on a held\-out adversarial set written afterward it produced **one** — a direct debit that is already carried as a Recurring Expense and swept by §3.11, recorded a second time. The lesson is not the count but its source: a veto list is *reactive*, and each new class of error is discovered by getting it wrong once, on real money. So the mechanism is admissible only because the result lands as a **pre\-filled quick\-add awaiting §3.6's Confirm**, where a bad read is a suggestion the person declines rather than a corrupted figure. Writing directly to `Category.spent` — a stored figure with no ledger to reconcile against (§4.3, §12) — is ruled out for every candidate. One app\-specific mitigation is available and should be taken: cross\-check a parsed payee against existing `RecurringExpense` names and suppress a match, which retires the one surviving error class outright.

**A cash withdrawal is an expense — moved to §3.2, 2026\-09\-05.** The question surfaced here, because it only becomes ambiguous once something other than the person is classifying an outflow. The ruling itself is a fact about the model rather than about any mechanism, so it lives with the rule it qualifies (§3.2) and is recorded there in full.

**Resolved 2026\-09\-06 — file import plus an opt\-in reminder, and nothing crosses the device boundary.** Of the six positions on the front, this one was chosen and built. The full mechanism is specified in §4.3; recorded here is why it won and what building it changed.

- **It was chosen over continuous notification parsing on the merits, not on caution.** The reminder position beat it on coverage *and* on cost at identical posture, which removed notification listening from the front entirely rather than ranking it second (§7.3's corrected basis records that it was a real option, not a policy casualty).
- **The register argument was independent of the score.** §2.6's automation finding is that the highest\-leverage fix is to stop asking rather than to ask more nicely; continuous parsing produces a stream of small confirmations, while this asks once a month and only if the person switched it on.
- **Building it surfaced one thing the analysis had not.** Deduplication was scored on OFX's `<FITID>` giving exact identity, which it does — but a CSV export and an OFX export *of the same period share no id*, so a person who switched formats would have re\-imported everything silently. The build therefore records **both** an id key and a synthetic date/amount/description key and checks both. That makes a different error possible — two genuinely separate identical purchases on one day now collide — and the trade is deliberate: a false duplicate arrives unticked and visible on the review screen, where a missed duplicate is a silent double\-count against Savings.

~~**Nothing in §13.1 is open.**~~ **Superseded by the 2026\-09\-24 set at the head of §13.1 (corrected 2026\-09\-25).** The standing limitation below (§9.3's resource list) is a maintenance obligation rather than a question, and remains as recorded.

~~2. **Whether the Appearance panel stays inline in Settings** (§4.7, opened 2026\-09\-03).~~ **Resolved 2026\-09\-04 — pushed to a spoke.** Both callers that argued for keeping it inline (§11.1's Currency landing, §11.6's stop 8) turned out to be better served by navigating into the sub\-screen than by keeping 658px one level up. Settings went from 1,288px to 708px, and the ~~deepest screen anywhere under it~~ **hub** is now 1.07 viewports, **and the deepest screen under it 1.17 — Appearance, §4.7's table (corrected 2026\-09\-25)**, at the default text scale against 2.9 before the restructure began.

~~2. **Sheet scaling against the person's text scale** (§11.1, opened 2026\-09\-14).~~ **Resolved the same day: the person's scale multiplies on top of the width scaling, and the row wraps rather than shrinking (§11.1).** Originally: §11.1's first sheet scales its text with the screen width, so a 320dp phone shows smaller cards and smaller text than a 360dp one; §2.5 separately lets the person choose a text scale of up to 1.5×. Not decided: whether a chosen `xl` survives on a small screen — the sheet's sizes scale with width, the person's scale multiplies on top, and the row wraps rather than shrinking below what they chose — or whether width wins outright. It affects one screen, once, and is left to the person's call when Stage 2 reaches it rather than settled here.


**Stage 3 — finishing what the spec still marked open (2026\-09\-14, night)**

- Scope set with the person before anything was built: release packaging (a debug\-key release and the MSI, no Play) waits for the stage after; Overtime (§10) stays deferred and security & lock stays a placeholder; the completion notice stays on §3.9's card; the two 360dp rendering fixes are to be reconsidered with options; the icon is to be mocked up in Affinity for them to pick; §4.5's bodies follow the research audit once it is in the folder. Order measured (`opt15`, fitness 0.923): spec reconciliation → accessibility semantics and low\-distraction mode → the Profiles tutorial stop → the two fixes' options → the Windows notification walk → the device walks → §9.3's topic → the scale/dark pass → the Learn bodies → the icon.
- This entry's first act: the status lines Stage 2 made false — §7.3, §8.1, §8.3, §8.4, and §13.2's Profiles and ledger rows — struck and dated.
- The icon (2026\-09\-15, taken out of order at the person's request while step 4's options waited): four marks drawn and rendered through Inkscape's command line, then the person's own Metamorphous lettering exported from Affinity — the export itself driven through Affinity's window — outlined, recoloured and shown beside them; they chose the lettering, white on Slate. Built for both hosts and verified on each; recorded in §2.5.
- Text size follows the OS setting on both platforms, with the in\-app row kept and defaulted to *System* (2026\-09\-15, §2.5); the Windows side needs the `TextScaleFactor` read, which step 8 builds.
- Scaling made continuous at the person's request (2026\-09\-15): every measurement proportional to the window's width on both platforms, the three `uiScale` steps withdrawn, `dp` literals declared defects for step 8 to remove (§2.5).
- Step 5, the Windows walk (2026\-09\-15): four defects no test had reached, fixed and re\-verified against the real OS (§11.5) — the BOM, the permission read, the Task Scheduler entries, the AUMID shortcut — plus the headless mode and first\-launch setup `main` had lacked. Every artefact the walk created on the machine was removed at the end. The walk tests stay gated.
- Step 4, the two 360dp fixes reconsidered (2026\-09\-15): the person kept the stacked delete prompt and added a confirmation step to both of its deletes (*Go back* / *Delete*); for Reports they asked whether four views were still required — they are (§4.6) — and then whether the labels could be renamed to fit one row at full size; measured, and *Income* / *Owed* chosen. Both recorded in §3.6 and §4.6; the shared delete composable has a render of each confirmation step.
- Step 6, the device walks (2026\-09\-23): §4.7's backup exported, `pm clear`ed and restored through the Storage Access Framework, §4.3's statement import driven through the picker with a vetoed row and a reassigned one, and the phone rebooted to read WorkManager's queue back. All four claims held (§11.5). The walk's real yield was elsewhere: the tour's overlay had been offset by the status bar since the edge\-to\-edge frame landed on 2026\-09\-15, leaving stop 2's Skip and Next under the navigation bar and a first run with no way forward (§11.6). A walk that finds nothing in what it aimed at and a blocker beside it is the argument for walking rather than testing, stated once here so it need not be re\-argued.
- Two onboarding gaps the same walk exposed, both put to the person rather than decided (2026\-09\-23): a fresh install had no route to a restore until a throwaway Profile existed → screen 1 gains a restore line; an onboarding killed mid\-flow left `onboardingComplete` false forever and §11.3's prompt permanently ineligible → the step reached is stored and the flow resumes (§11.1).
- Step 7, §9.3's Learn topic (2026\-09\-23): written from §9.3's own bullets, with the title, its place in §4.5's order and the nesting of the country list decided with the person before a line of it was coded. §9.2's passive note in the payoff panel stays plain text and does **not** link to it — the stage plan had said it would, and §9.3 calls that note "context, never a trigger" while ruling the topic out of both calculators, so a tappable note there would have read as the prompt the section exists to avoid. The source line names the CFPB's research without naming a report, because the build could not check one offline.
- Step 8 opened with every question asked at once, at the person's request, and the answers moved the step (2026\-09\-23). Settled: the phone's scale factor is width ÷ 360dp, floored at 0\.85, no ceiling, multiplying spacing and icon sizes **only** — which takes radii and strokes *off* the screen axis §2.5 had put them on; density multiplies with a 6dp floor on `u`; and the in\-app text\-size setting is removed entirely in favour of the OS value, reversing the 2026\-09\-15 decision to keep it with a *System* default. Reopened: §4.8, because the desktop is to be designed rather than reflowed. Two facts the survey turned up before any of it: `uiScale` **had never been built at all** — §2.5 described an axis the code did not have, so this is an introduction and not a correction — and the 95 `dp` literals under `ui/` sit in 20 files, not the 19 the 2026\-09\-15 audit counted.
- The order was re\-measured rather than carried over when §4.8 reopened (2026\-09\-23): nine items through the same permutation search (dependencies hard 0\.50, fan\-out 0\.25, risk\-early 0\.15, cheap\-early 0\.10), fitness 0\.821 with every dependency satisfied, found by Harmony Search at generation 13 rather than being the seed. The one placement hand\-ordering would have got wrong: the two\-pane repair moved from fourth to eighth, because it was given a dependency on the desktop's redesign — there is no point repairing a frame that may be deleted.

**Onboarding screen 1 reviewed with the person, not decided for them (2026\-09\-14)**

- The first screen's distribution and presentation had been carried forward from the prototype and marked "Stage 2" without a decision being put to the person; raised by them and reviewed the same day. Seven distributions were mocked up at phone size (`design/onboarding-options.html`) and scored under §2.4's register; the person chose a bottom sheet with one column and one row of four themes — option E3 (§11.1).
- The theme names *Wabi Sabi* and *Tidal Keep* became *Wabi* and *Tide* (§2.5), on measurement: the row's cards hold 52–61px for a name across 320–360dp and both two\-word names were 61–64px. The stored tokens are `wabi` and `tide` in the prototype and the native build alike; the prototype's earlier `wabisabi`/`tidalkeep` and the native build's earlier `tidal` are read as legacy values.
- The evening's build, step by step, asked before it decided: step 3's placeholder, text\-scale rule and first\-open ground; step 4's placement of the two Profile notes; step 7's step\-2 page and step\-4 exit; step 12's Learn bodies — all answered and recorded. Two rendering faults the phone frames found were fixed as faults, not redesigned: a three\-button delete row and a four\-way segment that could not fit 360dp.\n- Later the same day, the remaining onboarding decisions the Stage 1 build had taken silently were put to the person and settled: screen 1 gains "Name this profile" and a text box before the re\-added theme heading and caption; Continue waits for a name; the first open creates the first Profile from that screen (no auto\-created "Mine"); a second Profile runs screen 1 and the Currency landing only; `onboardingComplete` is per\-Profile (§11.1, §8.3, §12).
- ~~Left open, deliberately: how the sheet's width\-proportional sizing composes with §2.5's text scale (§13.1, item 2).~~ Resolved the same evening with the two other spots Stage 2's step 3 found silent — the placeholder and the first open's ground — each put to the person and recorded in §11.1.

**Two entries above have since been superseded, and are kept in place rather than rewritten (noted 2026\-09\-02).** A trail that quietly edits itself stops being a trail, so where a later decision overtook an earlier one the earlier entry stands and is flagged here:

- The 2026\-07\-21→07\-23 entry says **"§3.5 and §6.2 read the swept figure directly."** They now read the month's *scheduled* occurrence total instead, whether or not the sweep has fired (§3.5, §3.11 — 2026\-09\-02). The point that entry was making — that there is one figure rather than a second smoothed one computed for threshold purposes — still holds; only which figure changed.
- The 2026\-09\-02 entry describing pair\-deletion's recorded effect **"including `incomeReceivedThisMonth`"** was written earlier in the same pass than the decision to derive that figure rather than store it. §7.3 is correct: there is no decrement rule, because removing the rows removes them from the sum.

## 14\. Document Notes

Consolidated from `sci-fin-research-audit.md` (the original science\-backed audit of BudgetFlow, the app this project replaces), two iterations of an interactive HTML prototype, and an extended design conversation covering the financial model reframe (Savings as foundational primitive), navigation/register audit, Accounts scoping, quick\-add, onboarding revision, and the Loan/Profiles sketches. It is not a substitute for `sci-fin-prototype.html` — that remains the reference for pixel\-level layout and interaction flow; this document is the reference for *decisions, reasoning, and what's still open*. **The prototype\-copy rule was narrowed 2026\-09\-24 (§4.5):** prototype text may be adopted as a starting draft where each paragraph is then checked against the source this document names for it, and may never be adopted unchecked. The property being protected is traceability to a cited finding, not the identity of whoever drafted the sentence.

**Known prototype drift, audited item\-by\-item against the file 2026\-09\-03.** The previous version of this list opened by saying the prototype "has not been updated since the 2026\-07\-23 pass." That was wrong, and wrong in both directions. The file is a Stage 3 rebuild that had already absorbed five of the items listed against it, while other entries were real and two more went unlisted. A reader working from it would have been sent to rewrite code that was already correct, and would have missed a defect that inverted every Goal and Category row in the ledger. Every claim below was checked against the file rather than carried forward.

**Was never drift — removed from this list 2026\-09\-03.** The prototype already had all of these:

- **§7.1's** Checking/Cash removal and **§3.4's** Investment removal — zero references to either remain anywhere in the file.
- **§5.1's** real FIFO Age\-of\-Money engine — `computeAgeOfMoney` walks actual lots against actual Transactions; there was no "placeholder logic" to replace.
- **§3.3's** "no income logged" note — implemented, including the semimonthly anchor branch.
- **§2.1's** bar mechanics — the bar's extent is already `max(reference, actual)` with the tick at the reference and the hatch spanning reference→actual. Only two details were missing, and both are now closed.

**§7.3 is not drift either, and was miscategorised rather than merely overstated.** §7.3 is itself a Deferred section — "architecture decided, screen not built" — and the prototype has no ledger screen, only an Activity tab that says so in as many words. Pair\-deletion cannot be behind on a surface that neither document nor prototype has. It returns to this list when the ledger screen is built, not before.

**Closed 2026\-09\-03, in the pass that produced this audit:**

- **§2.5's visual system, in full.** `bordStrong` in all eight palettes and applied to every load\-bearing boundary; the tick redrawn in `txt` at the emphasis weight; eleven informational `txt3` uses promoted to `txt2`; and the geometry system — type ramp, spacing, radii, strokes, target floor — rebuilt on the three scaling axes, so no measurement in the app is a bare pixel constant and §4.8's size classes need no second set of numbers.
- **§5.3** — `savingsVsOwed` throughout, off by default, and the history is now a genuine forward replay of the ledger. It had been a flat line computed from current balances with a dip pasted on by hand; the demo ledger now seeds the opening rows and the loan's monthly principal decrements, so the dip is earned by the data.
- **§12's field set** — `firstDueDate`, `dueDateAnchorDay`, `intervalDays`, `apportionedShare`, `finishedAt`, `incomeEnabledDate`, `goalPromptDismissed`, `catchUpSummaryPending`, plus §6.1's `targetDate`, §9.1's amortization inputs including `rateType`, `Goal.mode`'s `pace` → `amount` rename, and `Savings.amount` made genuinely nullable. `incomeReceivedThisMonth` and `lastIncomeResetMonth` were removed in favour of deriving the figure on read.
- **§3.6's sign convention — found during this audit and not previously on any list.** The prototype stored the *Savings effect* rather than the delta to the named target, which is the exact inverted reading §3.6's table exists to forbid. Every Goal and Category row carried the wrong sign. It is now derived in one place from the key, and every ledger reader goes through it.
- **§3.6's Savings affordances** — the two separate quick\-add buttons, the direction control on Savings and on both Account types, and the Savings full editor with its typed\-UPDATE prompt and signed\-delta Transaction.
- **§7.2's Loan auto\-update** — the real interest/principal split recomputed from live state each cycle, replacing a flat illustrative 75%, and logging its own Transaction.
- **§6.4's rebased threshold**, **§4.6/§5.4's conditional Income vs Expenses**, **§2.1's tick at equality**, and **§2.2's difference caption** — the caption had been reading "€300 of €200 planned", which restates the reference instead of giving the difference §2.1 asks for.
- **§11.6's stop list** — it carried **nine** stops, not the eight §11.6 specifies: the dropped Profiles stop was still there, and stops 3 and 4 still held superseded copy, including the "detracts its full amount from Savings every 1st" line §11.6 corrected precisely because it teaches the wrong model of the app's most surprising mechanic.

**Register defects found and closed 2026\-09\-03 (second pass).** Raised on review — the app had stopped reading the way §2.3 and §2.4 describe. Four of the five were long\-standing rather than new, but §2.5's larger type made them impossible to miss:

- **The `feasibility-note` was drawn as a solid accent plate with `onAcc` text** — byte\-identical to `.btn-primary`, i.e. the accent at *maximum* emphasis, where §2.3 specifies **"the accent at reduced emphasis."** An ambient note attached to no action was the heaviest element on Budget → Envelopes, outweighing the Savings figure above it. Now an accent wash on the surface with ordinary text.
- **Both ambient notes carried closing language §2.3 forbids them.** §3.5's threshold note ended *"You can still manage it as-is"* — the clause §2.3 scopes to notes attached to something being saved, and calls incoherent on a standing note about nothing in particular. §3.3's "no income logged" note ended with a corrective instruction, *"quick-add it on Savings and check 'Income'"*, which also named a checkbox §3.6 had already replaced with two buttons. Both now state the fact and stop; the two notes inside the Goal calculator keep the clause, correctly, because there is something to save.
- **§3.5's expense side read swept\-to\-date, not the month's scheduled occurrence total** — the 2026\-09\-02 decision, never implemented. §6.2 read the same wrong figure. Both now use §3.11's count/apportionment math.
- **§3.5's check ran at `Savings: null`**, firing unconditionally at someone who had simply not entered a figure yet, because the accessor reads null as 0 for arithmetic. §3.1/§3.5 say the check does not run at all in that state.
- **The Income\-off placeholder wore the control\-weight dashed `bordStrong` edge** of the "\+ New goal / category / expense / account" affordances, so an inert placeholder read as something tappable. It now sits on `surf2` with a hairline dash — which also removes it from `bordStrong`'s stated condition, since its border is no longer the only thing defining its extent.

**Checked in the same pass and found correct:** no valence colour anywhere on a money state (a negative figure is drawn at reduced opacity, never in red); the only red in the app is the delete button on entity editors, a destructive\-action affordance rather than a verdict; no tone punctuation or grading vocabulary in any user\-facing string; low\-distraction mode can only hide icons explicitly marked decorative, so it cannot reach a figure, a caption, a note or the §2.1 hatch; and all five entity\-icon render sites are marked decorative as §2.5 requires.

**Closed 2026\-09\-04 — the three items this list carried as "deliberately stale":**

- **§4.8's desktop reflow is built.** All three rules and both named exceptions: the bottom bar becomes a navigation rail at 600dp, list\-detail opens a second pane at 840dp, bottom sheets become centred dialogs at 600dp, Reports and Learn stay single\-pane because §4.2's *Pushes to* cell gives them nothing to put in a second pane, and prose carries a measure cap. The width class is driven by a **ResizeObserver on the app's own frame, not a viewport media query** — the app is a box inside a review page here and a resizable window on a desktop, and in both cases what decides the layout is how wide the *app* is. §2.5's `uiScale` reference values ride the same classes, which is what finally connects that token to something. Verified across 3 frame widths × 4 text scales: no text ellipsised away, none outside its card, no horizontal scroll anywhere.
- **§3.9's catch\-up, §3.11's anchor chain and §3.11's edit\-and\-reconcile are built.** The month engine was a one\-shot "advance one month" path, so a person returning after three months had two of them silently skipped — the one thing §3.9 says must never happen. It is now driven by the gap between what was last processed and what month it actually is: every elapsed cycle replays in order with real historical dates, running it twice does nothing, and it is called on every time advance because §3.9's real trigger is opening the app. `catchUpSummaryPending` is set only when more than one month replayed, per §3.9's "only after a real absence." `dueDate` now advances when its date passes in the real world rather than when the sweep fires, floored at `firstDueDate`, with fixed\-date cadences re\-deriving the day from `dueDateAnchorDay` — so a 31st\-of\-the\-month rent snaps to 28 February and returns to 31 March instead of staying there, which is the defect §12 added that field to prevent.
- **§2.5's touch\-target floor now holds on every control.** The in\-row quick\-add button was the exception, on the grounds that a centred 48dp hit box inside a tappable row would swallow row taps. That is the trade the platform guidance itself makes — Android's `TouchDelegate` hands a child an expanded rect out of the parent's area — and deferring it protected a 15% edge case at the cost of everyone who cannot reliably hit a 25dp circle. The hit area expands leftward only, so it never crosses the scroll container and loses none of itself to clipping.

**Still open, and none of it drift:**

- **§7.3's ledger screen, §8's Profiles, and §9/§10's calculators** are Deferred by design in this document. A prototype cannot be behind on a screen neither document nor build has.
- ~~**§3.11's edit\-and\-reconcile flow, creation branches, and due\-date reminders.**~~ **All built 2026\-09\-04.** Edit\-and\-reconcile recomputes what the month would come to under the new settings, asks once with the difference and no default answer implied, dates the resulting Transaction **to the occurrence it adjusts** rather than to today so §3.11's un\-elapsed\-sum reversal test still applies, and moves a Loan\-linked Account's balance by the same amount as principal. All four creation branches now exist and all four set `lastSweptMonth`. Due\-date reminders are built to the platform seam. **Building the last two surfaced four defects that reading could not have:** the occurrence count was a table of constants for three of four short cadences, so `firstDueDate`, `endDate` and `dueDate2` were partly or wholly unread; every entity id was a bare `Date.now()`, so two entities created in the same millisecond shared an id and therefore shared their ledger history; the anchor chain ran only from the month\-boundary path, so opening the app a few days later left every due date stale; and `weekly` \+ `fixedDate` — the illegal state §3.11 names explicitly — was reachable in two taps in the editor.

- ~~**Each occurrence writing its own dated Transaction.**~~ **Built 2026\-09\-04**, and with it the ledger\-derived deletion reversal it was blocking. The demo ledger is now seeded from the same occurrence math as the engine rather than from a hand\-written list, so the seeded history cannot drift from the shape sweeps produce — the previous seed dated every row to the 1st whatever the real due date was, and skipped alternate months for the bimonthly expense, which apportionment does not do. Building it surfaced a fourth §3.11 defect: the edit\-and\-reconcile row was dated to the 1st rather than to the occurrence it adjusts, which defeated the un\-elapsed test that section explicitly ties it to.

- ~~**`apportionedShare`'s formula.**~~ **Built 2026\-09\-04**, together with the `dueDate`\-advance recompute it turned out to depend on — which was itself never built, and which the flat divisor had been hiding, since `amount ÷ 12` is the same number every cycle. Shipping the formula alone would have replaced a bounded one\-off under\-reservation with a permanent 3× over\-reservation. Verified across two full cycles: €1,200 reserved in each, where the old formula reserved €400 in the first.

**With that, every mechanic in §3.3, §3.5, §3.6, §3.9 and §3.11 is built.** ~~What remains unbuilt in this document is Deferred by design (§7.3's ledger screen, §8's Profiles, §9/§10's calculators), the two native notification schedulers behind §1's platform seam, and Learn's body copy (§4.5).~~

**That list is closed, 2026\-09\-05.** Every entry on it has since been built, and it is struck rather than edited because the sequence matters to anyone reading the trail: §7.3's ledger screen with its pair\-deletion rules; §8's Profiles at §8.4's single wrap point, with Transactions and Settings nesting in full and no `kind` field invented (§13.2); §9.1's Loan calculator, §9.2's two surfaces and §9.3's dated country list; §10's Learn topic and calculator; and §4.5's four current topics, written from §2.6's research grounding and reordered to the fixed order that section specifies — which also merged the two cards that were citing one finding twice.

**The native build exists, and Stage 1 of it is complete (2026\-09\-14).** `native/` is a Gradle project — `:shared` (Kotlin Multiplatform, Android + desktop JVM; Room; Compose Multiplatform), `:androidApp`, `:desktopApp` — pinned to the versions a sibling project already builds with on this machine, so **`gradlew --offline clean check :androidApp:assembleDebug` is the acceptance criterion and passes**: 142 engine tests plus a headless render of all eight palettes, and a debug APK that installs and runs on hardware, creating its database and registering its WorkManager entries on first open. The engine was written from this document; the prototype is the second oracle — `tools/export_fixture.mjs` runs its seeding headlessly and the parity tests require every figure to agree. Where the two disagreed the spec won, and the disagreements were the prototype's: JavaScript's `setMonth` overflow (31 January + 1 month landing in March, which §3.11's anchor rule already forbade) and floating\-point money. Stage 1 has **no screens** beyond a shell that proves §2.5's token layer on both hosts; §4's screens, §4.8's reflow, §11 and §4.5's copy are Stage 2.

~~**The two native schedulers are written but not compiled (stated precisely, 2026\-09\-05).**~~ **Compiled 2026\-09\-14, and the compiler found two defects the text could not:** kotlinx\-datetime 0.7 had moved `Clock` to `kotlin.time`, and `actual typealias PlatformContext = Context` is illegal because an `expect class` is final while `Context` is abstract — the Android side is now a one\-field holder, the thing the README had hoped to avoid. Originally: They live under `native/` as the `expect`/`actual` seam §1 commits to: `NotificationScheduler` as a shared interface with one construction point, WorkManager on Android, and WinRT scheduled toasts plus a Task Scheduler top\-up and a logon entry on Windows. **Android deliberately does not request `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM`/`USE_EXACT_ALARM`** — Play restricts the latter to alarm and clock apps, and §3.11's lead time is a whole number of days, so exactness was never the requirement; WorkManager also supplies §13.3's boot recovery for free. No Kotlin toolchain was available where they were written, so **they have not been compiled** and a first `./gradlew build` is their real verification. The `native/README.md` records this and the two integration prerequisites (an AppUserModelID for unpackaged Windows builds, and a headless `--sync-notifications` mode for the scheduled entries to invoke). **Both prerequisites are now met by the app itself and the seam was walked on a Windows 11 machine, 2026\-09\-15 (§11.5).**

**What that leaves genuinely open is §13.1's one live question and nothing else** *(resolved 2026\-09\-06; for what is open now see §13.1 — note added 2026\-09\-25)* — category automation from external data, where 2026\-09\-05's analysis narrowed the option space and scored it without deciding it.
- *(Done 2026\-09\-06, as the struck item below records — note added 2026\-09\-25.)* **§13.1's one live design question** — category automation from external data. It is the only item in this document with no mechanism proposed, and it needs a decision rather than an implementation; inventing one here would be exactly the kind of thing §14's build\-from\-spec rule exists to stop.

**Build\-from\-spec, not build\-from\-mockup (added 2026\-07\-28).** A prototype audit\-and\-fix pass surfaced the concrete risk this note exists to head off: the prototype carries its own bugs (a CSS flex sizing bug that shoved an editor's Save button off\-screen, an event\-propagation bug that silently ate every click inside the quick\-add modal, a reversal\-math counter that never incremented) and a full set of fictional demo data (goal names, dollar amounts, account balances, a seeded transaction history) that exists solely to make the mockup navigable. Neither category is a specification of anything, and both are the kind of detail that quietly becomes "real" if a code\-writing phase treats the prototype as a reference implementation instead of a visual and interaction reference. The rule: implementation is built from this document's Decided sections; the prototype is consulted only for pixel\-level layout and interaction\-flow detail, never for data values, defaults, or as a source to copy logic from wholesale. Anywhere this document is silent or ambiguous, that's a gap to resolve here — via §13.1 if it's a real open design question — not license to fall back on whatever the prototype happens to do.

**This rule is now fully meetable — for the second time, and this time for the whole visual system (2026\-09\-03).** On 2026\-09\-02 the palettes were transcribed into §2.5 with their measured ratios, the token set corrected from six to eleven, and the two failing pairs remedied rather than recorded, and this paragraph then claimed that nothing here required reading `sci-fin-prototype.html`. **That claim was too broad by exactly one category.** Colour was closed; *size* was not. The type ramp, every spacing value, every corner radius and every stroke weight still existed only in the prototype, and §2.5's two sentences on them — "applied to every text token proportionally" and "base spacing unit 8px / 6px" — named no tokens and gave no formula, which is why the prototype had drifted to a non\-proportional ramp and two unrelated spacing numbers without contradicting anything written here. §2.5's geometry system closes it: three scaling axes, seven type ratios, two spacing formulas, seven radius steps and three stroke weights, all as proportions rather than pixel lists, so **nothing in this document now requires reading `sci-fin-prototype.html` to build from — including §4.8's desktop layout, which the prototype does not implement at all.** The prototype remains useful for interaction\-flow detail, and remains barred as a source of values, defaults or logic.

**The document is now checked by a script, not by eye (added 2026\-09\-14).** `tools/speccheck.py` fails on four things that were previously caught only by someone noticing: a `§` reference to a section that does not exist, a backticked `name()` — a claim that something is built — naming a symbol that exists in neither `sci-fin-prototype.html` nor `native/`, a date written with bare hyphens rather than this file's escaped form, and two headings sharing a number. Other backticked identifiers that appear in no file are reported as warnings only, because §12's field names legitimately live in prose. **It does not prove that a section's prose matches what the code does** — a symbol existing is not a mechanism matching, and that judgement stays with the reader. `tools/selftest.py` builds a fixture from the real files, applies one known fault at a time and requires the checker to exit 1 *and* name the fault, with negative cases for a struck reference to a real section, an escaped date and a reference into the research audit; a checker that has only ever printed green is not known to work. Harvested from a sibling project's `spec:check`/`selftest` pair, whose author's finding was that inserting six lines silently slides every later citation and the document goes on reading as authoritative. Run after every edit to this file.
